“No, he claimed to have gotten it in trade from some wayfaring collector,” Gresham replied. “He convinced Uncle Whiskers, but the N.R.A. took a slightly dimmer view of the transaction, so Rivers doesn’t advertise in the *Rifleman* any more.”

===Chinese===
「不，他宣称是同一个云游四方的收藏者以货易货换来的，」格雷沙姆答道，「他把『大胡子大叔』（Uncle Whiskers）糊弄过去了，可全国步枪协会对这桩交易的观感却差了一截，于是里弗斯再不能在《美国步枪手》上登广告了。」

===Original===
“Wasn’t there some talk about Whitneyville Walker Colts that had been made out of 1848 Model Colt Dragoons?” Rand asked.

===Chinese===
「不是还有些传闻，说有用 1848 年型柯尔特龙骑兵枪（Colt Dragoon）改成的惠特尼维尔·沃克型柯尔特么？」兰德问。

===Original===
“Oh Lord, yes! This fellow Umholtz was practically turning them out on an assembly-line, for a while. Rivers must have sold about ten of them. You know, Umholtz is a really fine gunsmith; I had him build a deer-rifle for Dot, a couple of years ago—Mexican-Mauser action, Johnson barrel, chambered for .300 Savage; Umholtz made the stock and fitted a scope-sight—it’s a beautiful little rifle. I hate to see him prostitute his talents the way he does by making these fake antiques for Rivers. You know, he made one of these mythical heavy .44 six-shooters of the sort Colt was supposed to have turned out at Paterson in 1839 for Colonel Walker’s Texas Rangers—you know, the model he couldn’t find any of in 1847, when he made the real Walker Colt. That story you find in Sawyer’s book.”

===Chinese===
「老天爷，可不！有一阵子，这个乌姆霍尔茨差不多是在流水线上成批地造这路货。里弗斯怕是统共卖出去十来支。你知道，乌姆霍尔茨实在是个好枪匠；几年前我请他给多特造过一支猎鹿枪——墨西哥毛瑟枪机，约翰逊枪管，打 .300 萨维奇（.300 Savage）弹；枪托是乌姆霍尔茨自做的，又装了具瞄准镜——一支顶漂亮的小步枪。眼看他替里弗斯造这些假古董、把一身本事这样作践，我心里实在不是滋味。你知道，他还造过一支那种传说中的重型 .44 口径六响枪——据说柯尔特 1839 年在帕特森替沃克上校的得克萨斯骑警队造过的那一型号——你知道，就是 1847 年他造出真正的沃克型柯尔特时，一支也寻不着的那种。这个故事，你在索耶（Sawyer）的书里就读得到。」

===Original===
“Why, that story’s been absolutely disproved,” Rand said. “There never was any such revolver.”

===Chinese===
「嗐，那个说法早被彻底驳倒了，」兰德说，「压根儿就不曾有过那样一种转轮枪。」

===Original===
“Not till Umholtz made one,” Gresham replied. “Rivers sold it to,”﻿—he named a moving-picture bigshot﻿—“for twenty-five hundred dollars. His story was that he picked it up in Mexico, in 1938; traded a .38-special to some halfbreed goat-herder for it.”

===Chinese===
「乌姆霍尔茨造出一支之前，确实没有，」格雷沙姆答道。「里弗斯把它卖给了」——他说出了一位电影界大亨的名字——「两千五百美元。他讲的来历是：1938 年在墨西哥撞见的，拿一支 .38 特种型（.38 Special）同一个混血的牧山羊人换来的。」

===Original===
“This fellow who bought it, now; did he see Belden and Haven’s Colt book, when it came out in 1940?”

===Chinese===
「那么买下它的这位呢；贝尔登与黑文（Belden and Haven）那部柯尔特专著 1940 年问世的时候，他看到过没有？」

===Original===
“Yes, and he was plenty burned up, but what could he do? Rivers was dug in behind this innocent-purchase-and-sale-in-good-faith Maginot Line of his. You know, that bastard took me, once, just one-tenth as badly, with a fake U.S. North & Cheney Navy flintlock 1799 Model that had been made out of a French 1777 Model.” The lawyer muttered obscenities.

===Chinese===
「看到了，也气得七窍生烟，可他能怎么样？里弗斯深沟高垒，躲在他那条『不知情的善意买卖』的马其诺防线后面。你可知道，那个混蛋也坑过我一回，只不过狠劲只有十分之一——一支假的 1799 年型美国诺思与切尼（North & Cheney）海军燧发枪，拿法国 1777 年型改出来的。」律师低声骂了几句粗话。

===Original===
“Why didn’t you sue hell out of him?” Rand asked. “You might not have gotten anything, but you’d have given him a lot of dirty publicity. That’s all Fleming was expecting to do about those wheel locks.”

===Chinese===
「你怎么不往死里告他一状？」兰德问，「钱兴许一个子儿也要不回来，可也够他背一身臭名了。弗莱明当初想拿那两支转轮打火枪做的，也无非就是这一步。」

===Original===
“I’m not Fleming. He could afford litigation like that; I can’t. I want my money, and if I don’t get it in cash, I’m going to beat it out of that dirty little swindler’s hide,” Gresham replied, an ugly look appearing on his face.

===Chinese===
「我不是弗莱明。那样的官司他打得起，我打不起。我要的是我的钱；要是现款拿不到，我就要从那个下流小骗子的皮肉上，把它生生敲出来，」格雷沙姆答道，脸上浮起一股凶相。

===Original===
“I wouldn’t blame you. You could find plenty of other collectors who’d hold your coat while you were doing it,” Rand told him. Then he inquired, idly: “What sort of a pistol was it that Lane Fleming is supposed to have shot himself with?”

===Chinese===
「那我不怪你。真到那天，有的是旁的收藏家乐意替你按住外套，」兰德告诉他。随后他才闲闲地问：「莱恩·弗莱明据说用来把自己打死的那支手枪，是哪一路的？」

===Original===
Gresham frowned. “I really don’t know; I didn’t see it. It’s supposed to have been a Confederate Leech & Rigdon .36; you know, one of those imitation Colt Navy Models that were made in the South during the Civil War.”

===Chinese===
格雷沙姆皱起眉头。「我实在不知道；我没有见过。据说是支南方邦联的利奇与里格登（Leech & Rigdon）.36 口径——你知道，就是内战期间南方造的那些仿柯尔特海军型。」

===Original===
Rand nodded. He was familiar with the type.

===Chinese===
兰德点了点头。这一型号他是熟知的。

===Original===
“The story is that Fleming found it hanging back of the counter at some roadside lunch-stand, along with a lot of other old pistols, and talked the proprietor into letting it go for a few dollars,” Gresham continued. “It was supposed to have been loaded at the time, and went off while Fleming was working on it, at home.” He shook his head. “I can’t believe that, Jeff. Lane Fleming would know a loaded revolver when he saw one. I believe he deliberately shot himself, and the family faked the accident and fixed the authorities. The police never made any investigation; it was handled by the coroner alone. And our coroner, out in Scott County, is eminently fixable, if you go about it right; a pitiful little nonentity with a tremendous inferiority complex.”

===Chinese===
「据说弗莱明是在一处路边吃食摊的柜台后头撞见它的，同一大堆别的旧手枪挂在一起，几句好话，就让老板作价几个美元让了给他，」格雷沙姆接着说，「据称当时枪里上着弹药，弗莱明在家中摆弄时走了火。」他摇头。「这个我信不过，杰夫。莱恩·弗莱明那样的人，转轮枪里有没有子弹，他一搭眼就知道。我相信他是存心开枪打死了自己，家里人把『意外』做了假局，又把官府上下打点平了。警察从来没有做过任何调查；全凭验尸官一人经手。而我们那位验尸官——斯科特县（Scott County）的那一位——只要路数使得对，极易摆平：一个自卑感重得吓人的可悲小角色。」

===Original===
“But good Lord, why?” Rand demanded. “I never heard of Fleming having any troubles worth killing himself over.”

===Chinese===
「可是老天爷，为的什么呀？」兰德诘问，「我从来没听说弗莱明有过什么愁事，值得拿性命去抵。」

===Original===
Gresham lowered his voice. “Jeff, I’m not supposed to talk about this, but the fact is that I believe Fleming was about to lose control of the Premix Company,” he said. “I have, well, sources of inside information. This is in confidence, so don’t quote me, but certain influences were at work, inside the company, toward that end.” He inspected the tip of his cigar and knocked off the ash into the tray at his elbow. “Lane Fleming’s death is on record as accidental, Jeff. It’s been written off as such. It would be a great deal better for all concerned if it were left at that.”

===Chinese===
格雷沙姆压低了嗓音。「杰夫，这事我本不该讲；可实情是，我相信弗莱明当时眼看要保不住普雷米克斯公司的控制权了，」他说，「我有，呃，一些内部消息的门路。这话是私谊相告，别引我具名；公司内部有某些势力，正朝这个方向使着劲。」他端详了端详雪茄的烟头，把烟灰磕进手肘边的烟灰缸。「莱恩·弗莱明的死，案卷上记的是意外，杰夫。已经照此销案了。就这么放着不动，于有关各方都要好得多。」

## V / 五

===Original===
Rand drove slowly through Rosemont, the next day, refreshing his memory of the place. It was one of the many commuters’ villages strung out for fifty miles along the railroad lines radiating from New Belfast, and depended for its support upon a population scattered over a five-mile radius at estates and country homes. Obviously a planned community, it was dominated by a gray-walled, green-roofed railroad station which stood on its passenger-platform like a captain in front of four platoons of gray-walled, green-roofed houses and stores aligned along as many converging roads. There was a post office, uniform with the rest of the buildings; an excessive quantity of aluminum trimming dated it somewhere in the middle Andrew W. Mellon period. There were four gas stations, a movie theater, and a Woolworth store with a red front that made it look like some painted hussy who had wandered into a Quaker Meeting.

===Chinese===
第二天，兰德驱车缓缓穿过罗斯蒙特，重温着对这地方的记忆。这是沿新贝尔法斯特辐射而出的各条铁路线、绵延五十英里排开的许多通勤村镇之一，靠方圆五英里内散居在庄园乡邸里的人家撑持。这地方显然是个经过规划的社区，坐镇其中是一座灰墙绿顶的火车站，它立在自家的旅客站台上，活像一名上尉站在四排灰墙绿顶的房屋店铺跟前——那些建筑顺着同样数目的道路汇聚排开。有一所邮局，与其余建筑如出一辙；铝质装饰用量之滥，把它定在了安德鲁·W·梅隆（Andrew W. Mellon）时期的中段。四座加油站，一家电影院，还有一家红门脸的伍尔沃思（Woolworth）商店——那副模样，活像个浓妆艳抹的轻佻女人，误闯进了贵格会（Quaker）的礼拜会。

===Original===
Over the door of one of the smaller stores, Rand saw a black-lettered white sign: *Antiques*. There was a smoke-gray Plymouth coupé parked in front of it.

===Chinese===
在一家较小店铺的门楣上方，兰德望见一块黑字白底的招牌：古董（Antiques）。门前停着一辆烟灰色的普利茅斯（Plymouth）双门轿车。

===Original===
Instead of turning onto the road to the Fleming estate, he continued along Route 19 for a mile or so beyond the village, until he came to a redbrick pseudo-Colonial house on the right. He pulled to the side of the road and got out, turning up the collar of his trench coat. The air was raw and damp, doubly unpleasant after the recent unseasonable warmth. An apathetically persistent rain sogged the seedling-dotted old fields on either side, and the pinewoods beyond, and a high ceiling of unbroken dirty gray gave no promise of clearing. The mournful hoot of a distant locomotive whistle was the only sound to pierce the silence. For a moment, Rand stood with his back to the car, looking at the gallows-like sign that proclaimed this to be the business-place of Arnold Rivers, Fine Antique and Modern Firearms for the Discriminating Collector.

===Chinese===
他没有拐上通往弗莱明家庄园的那条路，而是沿 19 号公路驶过村子又是一英里上下，直到右手边出现一幢红砖砌的仿殖民时期式样宅子。他把车靠到路边下来，竖起了风衣领子。空气阴冷潮湿，前几天那阵反季节的暖意过后，加倍叫人难受。一场没精打采却下个不停的雨，把两侧点缀着幼苗的旧田和更远处的松林都浇得透湿；高高一层浑然不断的脏灰天幕，毫无放晴的指望。远处机车汽笛一声哀鸣，是划破寂静的唯一声响。兰德背靠着车站了一会儿，望着那块绞刑架似的招牌——招牌上写明，此处是阿诺德·里弗斯的营业之所：为独具慧眼的收藏家，供应精美的古今枪械。

===Original===
The house faced the road with a long side; at the left, a porch formed a continuation under a deck roof, and on the right, an ell had been built at right angles, extending thirty feet toward the road. Although connected to the house by a shed roof, which acquired a double pitch and became a gable roof where the ell projected forward, it was, in effect, a separate building, with its own front door and its own door-path. Its floor-level was about four feet lower than that of the parent structure.

===Chinese===
宅子以长长的侧面朝向公路；左边，一道平顶游廊顺势延展，右边则成直角接出一爿厢房，朝公路方向探出三十英尺。厢房虽然以一面单坡顶与主宅相连——那屋顶在厢房前伸之处折起双坡，变成人字顶——实际上却是栋独立的建筑，有自己的正门和自己的门前小径。它的地面比主宅低四英尺光景。

===Original===
A Fibber McGee door-chime clanged as Rand entered. Closing the door behind him, he looked around. The room, some twenty feet wide and fifty long, was lighted by an almost continuous row of casement windows on the right, and another on the left for as far as the ell extended beyond the house. They were set high, a good five feet from lower sill to floor, and there was no ceiling; the sloping roof was supported by bare timber rafters. Racks lined the walls, under the windows, holding long-guns and swords; the pistols and daggers and other small items were displayed on a number of long tables. In the middle of the room, glaring at the front door, was a brass four-pounder on a ship’s carriage; a Philippine *lantaka*, muzzle tilted upward, stood beside it. Where the ell joined the house under the shed roof, there was a fireplace, and a short flight of steps to a landing and a door out of the dwelling, and some furniture﻿—a davenport, three or four deep chairs facing the fire, a low cocktail-table, a cellarette, and, in the far corner, a big desk.

===Chinese===
兰德进门时，一只《菲伯·麦吉》（Fibber McGee）式的门铃当啷一响。他随手带上门，环顾四周。这间屋子约莫二十英尺宽、五十英尺长，靠右侧一排几乎连成一线的竖铰链窗采光，左侧也同样开着一排，一直开到厢房探出主宅的尽处。窗子开得很高，窗台下沿离地足有五英尺；屋里不吊顶，斜屋面由裸露的木檩承着。窗下的墙面排满枪架，架上是长枪和刀剑；手枪、匕首及其他小件则摆开在几张长桌上。屋子正中，一门架在舰炮炮架上的黄铜四磅炮虎视眈眈地瞪着正门；旁边立着一门菲律宾兰塔卡（lantaka）回旋炮，炮口斜指着天。在厢房以单坡顶与主宅相接的地方，有一座壁炉，一小段台阶通上一方平台和一扇从宅子里通出来的门，另有几件家具——一张矮长沙发，三四把朝着炉火的深椅，一张低矮的鸡尾酒桌，一只酒柜，还有远处角落里一张大写字台。

===Original===
As Rand went toward the rear, a young man rose from one of the chairs, laid aside a magazine, and advanced to meet him. He didn’t exactly harmonize with all the lethal array around him; he would have looked more at home presiding over an establishment devoted to ladies’ items. His costume ran to pastel shades, he had large and soulful blue eyes and prettily dimpled cheeks, and his longish blond hair was carefully disordered into a windblown effect.

===Chinese===
兰德往店堂后头走时，一个年轻人从椅子里起身，搁下手里的杂志，迎上前来。他同四周这片致命的阵列实在不大协调；若是一家经营女士用品的铺面，由他坐镇才更般配。他的衣着一色素淡的柔彩，一双大而含情的蓝眼睛，一对好看的酒窝，一头略长的金发，被精心打理成风吹过的凌乱模样。

===Original===
“Oh, good afternoon,” he greeted. “Is there anything in particular you’re interested in, or would you like to just look about?”

===Chinese===
「哦，下午好，」他招呼道，「您有什么特别中意的东西吗，还是想随便看看？」

===Original===
“Mostly look about,” Rand said. “Is Mr. Rivers in?”

===Chinese===
「多半是随便看看，」兰德说，「里弗斯先生在吗？」

===Original===
“Mr. Rivers is having luncheon. He’ll be finished before long, if you care to wait.﻿ ﻿… Have you ever been here before?”

===Chinese===
「里弗斯先生正在用午餐。他快用完了，您若愿意稍候。……您以前来过吗？」

===Original===
“Not for some time,” Rand said. “When I was here last, there was a young fellow named Jordan, or Gordon, or something like that.”

===Chinese===
「有些日子没来了，」兰德说，「我上回来的时候，店里有个年轻人，叫乔丹还是戈登来着。」

===Original===
“Oh. He was before my time.” The present functionary introduced himself as Cecil Gillis. Rand gave his name and shook hands with him. Young Gillis wanted to know if Rand was a collector.

===Chinese===
「哦，那是我来之前的事了。」眼下这位当值的自报了名号：塞西尔·吉利斯（Cecil Gillis）。兰德报了姓名，同他握了手。小吉利斯又打听兰德是不是收藏家。

===Original===
“In a small way. General-pistol collector,” Rand told him. “Have you many Colts, now?”

===Chinese===
「小打小闹罢了。各型手枪通收，」兰德告诉他，「贵店如今柯尔特多吗？」

===Original===
There was a whole table devoted to Colts. No spurious Whitneyville Walkers; after all, a dealer can sell just so many of such top-drawer rarities before the finger of suspicion begins leveling itself in his direction, and Arnold Rivers had long ago passed that point. There were several of the commoner percussion models, however, with lovely, perfect bluing that was considerably darker than that applied at the Colt factory during the ’fifties and ’sixties of the last century. The silver plating on backstraps and trigger-guards was perfect, too, but the naval-battle and stagecoach-holdup engravings on the cylinders were far from clear﻿—in one case, completely obliterated. The cylinder of one 1851 Navy bore serial numbers that looked as though they had been altered to conform to the numbers on other parts of the weapon. Many of the Colts, however, were entirely correct, and all were in reasonably good condition.

===Chinese===
有一整张桌子专门陈列柯尔特。没有假冒的惠特尼维尔沃克（Whitneyville Walker）；毕竟，这种顶级行情的稀罕货，一个商人卖出那么几件，怀疑的手指就该开始指到他身上了，而阿诺德·里弗斯早在多年前就越过了那道坎。不过，桌上倒有几支较常见的火帽击发型，一身漂亮完好的烤蓝，比上世纪五六十年代柯尔特工厂所上的原蓝深了不少。握把背板和扳机护圈上的镀银也毫无瑕疵，只是转轮上那些海战和劫驿车的雕纹远算不得清晰——有一支干脆磨蚀殆尽。一支 1851 型海军转轮枪的转轮上，编号瞧着像是被动过，改得与枪身其他部件的编号对上了榫。话虽如此，这批柯尔特多数件件原装真确，且品相都还过得去。

===Original===
Rand saw something that interested him, and picked it up.

===Chinese===
兰德瞧见一件中意的东西，拿了起来。

===Original===
“That isn’t a real Colt,” the exquisite Mr. Gillis told him. “It’s a Confederate copy; a Leech & Rigdon.”

===Chinese===
「那可不是正宗柯尔特，」这位精致考究的吉利斯先生告诉他，「那是邦联的仿制品；一支利奇与里格登（Leech & Rigdon）。」

===Original===
“So I see. I have a Griswold & Grier, but no Leech & Rigdon.”

===Chinese===
「我看出来了。我有一支格里斯沃尔德与格里尔（Griswold & Grier），只是没有利奇与里格登。」

===Original===
“The Griswold & Grier; that’s the one with the brass frame,” Cecil Gillis said. “Surprising how many collectors think all Confederate revolvers had brass frames, because of the Griswold & Grier, and the Spiller & Burr.﻿ ﻿… That’s an unusually fine specimen, Mr. Rand. Mr. Rivers got it sometime in late December or early January; from a gentleman in Charleston, I understand. I believe it had been carried during the Civil War by a member of the former owner’s family.”

===Chinese===
「格里斯沃尔德与格里尔，黄铜枪身的那款，」塞西尔·吉利斯说，「真叫人意外，多少收藏家以为邦联转轮枪全都是黄铜枪身，就因为格里斯沃尔德与格里尔，还有斯皮勒与伯尔（Spiller & Burr）。……这支可是格外难得的好标本，兰德先生。里弗斯先生是去年十二月底、要不就是一月初到手的；据我所知，得自查尔斯顿（Charleston）的一位先生。我想，南北战争期间，它曾被原主人家族的一位成员随身携带。」

===Original===
Rand looked at the tag tied to the trigger-guard; it was marked, in letter-code, with three different prices. That was characteristic of Arnold Rivers’s business methods.

===Chinese===
兰德看了看拴在扳机护圈上的标签；上面以字母暗码标着三个不同的价钱。这是阿诺德·里弗斯生意经的固有做派。

===Original===
“How much does Mr. Rivers want for this?” he asked, handing the revolver to young Gillis.

===Chinese===
「里弗斯先生这支要价几何？」他问着，把转轮枪递给小吉利斯。

===Original===
The clerk mentally decoded the three prices and vacillated for a moment over them. He had already appraised Rand, from his twenty-dollar Stetson past his Burberry trench coat to his English hand-sewn shoes, and placed him in the pay-dirt bracket; however, from some remarks Rand had let drop, he decided that this customer knew pistols, and probably knew values.

===Chinese===
店员在心里破译出那三个价钱，一时委决不下。他早已把兰德从头到脚估量了一遍——从那顶二十美元的斯泰森（Stetson）帽，到身上的博柏利（Burberry）风衣，再到脚上那双英国手工缝制的皮鞋——把他归进了出油水的一档；不过，听兰德顺口漏出的几句言语，他断定这位主顾懂手枪，多半也懂行情。

===Original===
“Why, that is sixty dollars, Mr. Rand,” he said, with the air of one conferring a benefaction. Maybe he was, at that, Rand decided; prices had jumped like the very devil since the war.

===Chinese===
「哦，这支嘛，是六十美元，兰德先生，」他说，那副口气，仿佛在赏人恩惠。兴许还真是，兰德心想；打战争以来，物价涨得没了谱。

===Original===
“I’ll take it.” He dug out his billfold and extracted three twenties. “Nice clean condition; clean it up yourself?”

===Chinese===
「我要了。」他掏出皮夹，抽出三张二十元。「品相真干净；是你们自己收拾的？」

===Original===
“Why, no. Mr. Rivers got it like this. As I said, it’s supposed to have been a family heirloom, but from the way it’s been cared for, I would have thought it had been in a collection,” the clerk replied. “Shall I wrap it for you?”

===Chinese===
「哦，不是。里弗斯先生到手时就是这副样子。我说过，这枪据说原是件传家宝；可瞧它受保养的这架势，我还当它打哪家收藏里出来的呢，」店员答道，「要给您包起来吗？」

===Original===
“Yes, if you please.” Rand followed him to the rear, laying aside his coat and hat. Gillis got some heavy paper out of a closet and packaged it, then hunted through a card-file in the top drawer of the desk, until he found the card he wanted. He made a few notes on it, and was still holding it and the sixty dollars when he rejoined Rand by the fire.

===Chinese===
「好的，有劳。」兰德随他走到店堂后部，把外套和帽子搁在一边。吉利斯从壁橱里取出厚纸，将枪包好，又在写字台顶屉的卡片柜里翻检，直到找出他要的那张卡片。他在卡上记了几笔，手里还捏着卡片和那六十美元，回到炉火边兰德身旁。

===Original===
In spite of his effeminate appearance and overrefined manner, the young fellow really knew arms. The conversation passed from Confederate revolvers to the arms of the Civil War in general, and they were discussing the changes in tactics occasioned by the introduction of the revolver and the repeating carbine when the door from the house opened and Arnold Rivers appeared on the landing.

===Chinese===
别看他女人气的仪表和过分讲究的举止，这年轻人对兵器是真懂行。谈话从邦联转轮枪转到南北战争兵器的总体，两人正讨论转轮手枪与连发卡宾枪的引入给战术带来的变化，宅子那边的门开了，阿诺德·里弗斯出现在台阶平台上。

===Original===
He looked older than when Rand had last seen him. His hair was thinner on top and grayer at the temples. Never particularly robust, he had lost weight, and his face was thinner and more hollow-cheeked. His mouth still had the old curve of supercilious insolence, and he was still smoking with the six-inch carved ivory cigarette-holder which Rand remembered.

===Chinese===
他看上去比兰德上次见他时老了。头顶头发更稀，鬓角更灰。他向来算不上壮实，如今又掉了肉，脸更瘦，颊更陷。嘴上仍挂着那道傲慢无礼的老弧线，手里仍用着那支六英寸的雕花象牙烟嘴——兰德记得它。

===Original===
He looked his visitor over carefully from the doorway, decided that he was not soliciting magazine subscriptions or selling Fuller brushes, and came down the steps. As he did, he must have recognized Rand; he shifted the cigarette-holder to his left hand and extended his right.

===Chinese===
他从门口将来客细细打量一遍，断定此人既不是来揽杂志订户的，也不是上门推销富勒（Fuller）刷子的，便走下台阶。下台阶时，他多半认出了兰德；他把烟嘴换到左手，伸出右手来。

===Original===
“Mr. Rand, isn’t it?” he asked. “I thought I knew you. It’s been some years since you’ve been around here.”

===Chinese===
「是兰德先生吧？」他问，「我就觉得面熟。您有些年头没到这一带来了。」

===Original===
“I’ve been a lot of places in the meantime,” Rand said.

===Chinese===
「其间我到过不少地方，」兰德说。

===Original===
“You were here last in October, ’41, weren’t you?” Rivers thought for a moment. “You bought a Highlander, then. By Alexander Murdoch, of Doune, wasn’t it?”

===Chinese===
「您上回来是 1941 年 10 月，对吧？」里弗斯想了想。「那次您买了一支高地手枪（Highlander）。杜恩的亚历山大·默多克（Alexander Murdoch）做的，是吧？」

===Original===
“No; Andrew Strahan, of Edzel,” Rand replied.

===Chinese===
「不对；是埃德泽尔的安德鲁·斯特拉恩（Andrew Strahan），」兰德答道。

===Original===
Rivers snapped his fingers. “That’s right! I sold both of those pistols at about the same time; a gentleman in Chicago got the Murdoch. The Strahan had a star-pierced lobe on the hammer. Did you ever get anybody to translate the Gaelic inscription on the barrel?”

===Chinese===
里弗斯捻了个响指。「这就对上了！那两支枪是我差不多同时脱手的；默多克那支归了芝加哥的一位先生。斯特拉恩那支的击锤上，有个透雕星纹的圆饰。枪管上那行盖尔文题铭，您后来请人译出来过吗？」

===Original===
“You’ve a memory like Jim Farley,” Rand flattered. “The inscription was the clan slogan of the Camerons; something like: *Sons of the hound, come and get flesh!* I won’t attempt the original.”

===Chinese===
「您这记性，赛过吉姆·法利（Jim Farley），」兰德奉承道。「那题铭是卡梅伦氏族的战号，大意是：『猎犬的儿郎们，快来吃肉！』盖尔语原文，我就不敢献丑了。」

===Original===
“Mr. Rand just bought 6524, the Leech & Rigdon .36,” Gillis interjected, handing Rivers the card and the money. Rivers looked at both, saw how much Rand had been taken for, and nodded.

===Chinese===
「兰德先生刚买下 6524 号，那支 0.36 口径的利奇与里格登，」吉利斯插话，把卡片和钱递给里弗斯。里弗斯两样都看了看，明白兰德挨了多深的一刀，点了点头。

===Original===
“A nice item,” he faintly praised, as though anything selling for less than a hundred dollars was so much garbage. “Considering the condition in which Confederate arms are usually found, it’s really first-rate. I think you’ll like it, Mr. Rand.”

===Chinese===
「件不赖的货色，」他轻描淡写地夸了一句，仿佛凡卖价不足百元的都是破烂。「就邦联枪械通常寻获时的品相而论，这支确属一流。我想您会喜欢它的，兰德先生。」

===Original===
The telephone rang, Cecil Gillis answered it, listened for a moment, and then said: “For you, Mr. Rivers; long distance from Milwaukee.”

===Chinese===
电话铃响，塞西尔·吉利斯接了，听了一会儿，说道：「您的电话，里弗斯先生；密尔沃基（Milwaukee）来的长途。」

===Original===
Rivers’s face lit with the beatific smile of a cat at a promising mouse-hole. “Ah, excuse me, Mr. Rand.” He crossed to the desk, picked up the phone and spoke into it. “This is Arnold Rivers,” he said, much as Edward Murrow used to say, *This﻿—is London!* The telephone sputtered for a moment. “Ah, yes indeed, Mr. Verral. Quite well, I thank you. And you?﻿ ﻿… No, it hasn’t been sold yet. Do you wish me to ship it to you?﻿ ﻿… On approval; certainly.﻿ ﻿… Of course it’s an original flintlock; I didn’t list it as re-altered, did I?﻿ ﻿… No, not at all; the only replacement is the small spring inside the patchbox.﻿ ﻿… Yes, the rifling is excellent.﻿ ﻿… Of course; I’ll ship it at once.﻿ ﻿… Goodbye, Mr. Verral.”

===Chinese===
里弗斯脸上焕发出至福的微笑，活像一只蹲守在有指望的鼠洞口的猫。「啊，失陪片刻，兰德先生。」他踱到写字台前，拿起话筒开了口：「我是阿诺德·里弗斯。」那腔调，像极了爱德华·默罗（Edward Murrow）当年的报法——这里是——伦敦！电话里噼啪响了一阵。「啊，正是，维拉尔（Verral）先生。很好，谢谢您。您呢？……不，还没卖出。您要我寄给您吗？……看货定购，当然可以。……那自然是原装燧发；我没有把它列成后改燧发吧？……不，完全没有；唯一的换件，是补片盒里那根小弹簧。……是的，膛线极好。……当然；我马上发货。……再见，维拉尔先生。」

===Original===
He hung up and turned to his hireling, fairly licking his chops.

===Chinese===
他挂上电话，转向他的雇员，那副神情，简直像在咂嘴舔唇。

===Original===
“Cecil, Mr. Verral, in Milwaukee, whose address we have, has just ordered 6288, the F. Zorger flintlock Kentuck. Will you please attend to it?”

===Chinese===
「塞西尔，密尔沃基的那位维拉尔先生——地址咱们手上有——刚订了 6288 号，那支 F. 佐尔格（F. Zorger）的燧发肯塔基步枪。请你办一下。」

===Original===
“Right away, Mr. Rivers.” Gillis went to one of the racks under the windows and selected a long flintlock rifle, carrying it out the door at the rear.

===Chinese===
「这就去，里弗斯先生。」吉利斯走到窗下一座枪架前，挑出一支长长的燧发步枪，从后门搬了出去。

===Original===
“I issued a list, a few days ago,” Rivers told Rand. “When Cecil comes back, I’ll have him get you a copy. I’ve been receiving calls ever since; this is the twelfth long-distance call since Tuesday.”

===Chinese===
「几天前我发出了一份货单，」里弗斯对兰德说，「塞西尔回来后，我叫他给您取一份。打那以后电话就没断过；这是星期二以来的第十二个长途。」

===Original===
“Business must be good,” Rand commented. “I understand you’ve offered to buy the Lane Fleming collection. For ten thousand dollars.”

===Chinese===
「生意想必兴隆，」兰德说，「听说您出价要买莱恩·弗莱明那批收藏。一万美元。」

===Original===
“Where did you hear that?” Rivers demanded, looking up from the drawer in which he was filing the card on the Leech & Rigdon.

===Chinese===
「您从哪儿听来的？」里弗斯追问，从抽屉上抬起头来——他正把利奇与里格登那张卡片往抽屉里归档。

===Original===
“From Mrs. Fleming.” Rand released a puff of pipe smoke and watched it draw downward into the fireplace. “I’ve been retained to handle the sale of that collection; naturally, I’d know who was offering how much.”

===Chinese===
「弗莱明太太说的。」兰德吐出一口烟斗的烟，望着它被往下吸进壁炉。「那批收藏的出售已委托我经办；谁在出价、出多少，我自然该知道。」

===Original===
Rivers’s eyes narrowed. He came around the desk, loading another cigarette into his holder.

===Chinese===
里弗斯眯起眼。他从写字台后绕出来，往烟嘴里又装上一支烟。

===Original===
“And just why, might I ask, did Mrs. Fleming think it in order to employ a detective in a matter like that?” he wanted to know.

===Chinese===
「那么请问，」他偏要问明白，「这么一桩事，弗莱明太太怎么会认为合该雇个侦探？」

===Original===
Rand let out more smoke. “She didn’t. She employed an arms-expert, a Colonel Jefferson Davis Rand, U.S.A., O.R.C., who is a well-known contributor to the *American Rifleman* and the *Rifleman* and *Antiques* and the old *Gun Report*. You’ve read some of his articles, I believe?”

===Chinese===
兰德又吐出一口烟。「她没有。她雇的是一位兵器专家，美国陆军军官后备队（O.R.C.）的杰斐逊·戴维斯·兰德上校——他是《美国步枪手》（American Rifleman）和《步枪手》、《古董》（Antiques）以及老《枪械报告》（Gun Report）的知名撰稿人。他的文章，想必您读过几篇？」

===Original===
“Then you’re not making an investigation?”

===Chinese===
「那您不是在着手调查了？」

===Original===
“What in the world is there to investigate?” Rand asked. “I’m just selling a lot of old pistols for the Fleming estate.”

===Chinese===
「这世上到底有什么可调查的？」兰德反问，「我不过替弗莱明家的遗产出一批旧手枪罢了。」

===Original===
“I thought Fred Dunmore was doing that.”

===Chinese===
「我还当这事是弗雷德·邓莫尔在办。」

===Original===
“So did Fred. You’re both wrong, though. I am.” He got out Goode’s letter of authorization and handed it to Rivers, who read it through twice before handing it back. “You see anything in that about Fred Dunmore, or any of the other relatives-in-law?” he asked.

===Chinese===
「弗雷德也这么当来着。不过你们俩都错了。是我在办。」他掏出古德的那封委托书递给里弗斯，里弗斯从头到尾读了两遍才递还。「里头哪一句提到弗雷德·邓莫尔，或者哪一位别的姻亲了？」他问。

===Original===
“Well, I didn’t understand; I’m glad to know what the situation really is.” Rivers frowned. “I thought you were making some kind of an investigation, and as I’m the only party making any serious offer to buy those pistols, I wanted to know what there was to investigate.”

===Chinese===
「唉，是我没弄明白；得知实情，甚好。」里弗斯皱起眉头。「我原以为您在搞什么调查；而那批手枪，正经出价要买的眼下只有我一家，我自然想知道有什么可调查的。」

===Original===
“Do you consider ten thousand dollars to be a serious offer?” Rand asked. “And aren’t you forgetting Stephen Gresham and his friends?”

===Chinese===
「您认为一万美元算正经出价？」兰德问，「再说，您是不是把斯蒂芬·格雷沙姆和他那班朋友忘了？」

===Original===
“Oh, those people!” Rivers scoffed. “Mr. Rand, you certainly don’t expect them to be able to handle anything like this, do you?”

===Chinese===
「哦，那起人！」里弗斯嗤之以鼻。「兰德先生，您总不至于指望那起人办得了这路买卖吧？」

===Original===
“Well, the banks speak well of them,” Rand replied. “Some of them have good listings in Dun & Bradstreet’s, too.”

===Chinese===
「话虽如此，银行界对他们的口碑不错，」兰德答道，「他们中间有几位在邓白氏（Dun & Bradstreet）的资信记录也不坏。」

===Original===
“Well, so do I,” Rivers reported. “I can top any offer that crowd makes. What do you expect to get out of them, anyhow?”

===Chinese===
「我也不坏，」里弗斯声明，「那起人出什么价，我都能出得更高。您到底指望从他们那儿落着多少？」

===Original===
“I haven’t talked price with them, yet. A lot more than ten thousand dollars, anyhow.”

===Chinese===
「我还没跟他们谈价钱。反正，比一万美元多得多。」

===Original===
Rivers forced a laugh. “Now, Mr. Rand! That was just an opening offer. I thought Fred Dunmore was handling the collection.” He grimaced. “What do you think it’s really worth?”

===Chinese===
里弗斯强笑一声。「得了，兰德先生！那不过是开个价。我还当经手这批收藏的是弗雷德·邓莫尔。」他做了个苦脸。「您估它到底值多少？」

===Original===
Rand shrugged. “It probably has a dealer’s piece-by-piece list-value of around seventy thousand. I’m not nuts enough to expect anything like that in a lump sum, but please, let’s not mention ten thousand dollars in this connection any more. That’s on the order of Lawyer Marks bidding seventy-five cents for Uncle Tom; it’s only good for laughs.”

===Chinese===
兰德耸耸肩。「照商人逐件开列的牌价，它大概值七万上下。我还没疯到指望谁一口付足这个数；不过拜托，这档子事往后别再提一万美元了。那就好比马克斯律师出七毛五分钱买汤姆叔叔——只配博人一笑。」

===Original===
“Well, how much more than that do you think Gresham and his crowd will offer?”

===Chinese===
「那么您估计，格雷沙姆和他那班人肯出的价，能比这高出多少？」

===Original===
“I haven’t talked price with them, yet,” Rand repeated. “I mean to, as soon as I can.”

===Chinese===
「我还没跟他们谈价钱，」兰德又说了一遍，「我打算尽快去谈。」

===Original===
“Well, you get their offer, and I’ll top it,” Rivers declared. “I’m willing to go as high as twenty-five thousand for that collection; they won’t go that high.”

===Chinese===
「那好，您拿到他们的报价，我出得更高，」里弗斯宣布，「这批收藏我肯出到两万五；他们出不到那个数。」

===Original===
Although he just managed not to show it, Rand was really surprised. Even a consciousness of abstracting had not prepared him for the shock of hearing Arnold Rivers raise his own offer to something resembling an acceptable figure. A good case, he reflected, could be made of that for the actuality of miracles.

===Chinese===
兰德虽然好不容易才没露在脸上，心里确乎吃了一惊。饶是他素来明白自己的认识无非层层抽绎，冷不丁听见阿诺德·里弗斯把自己的报价抬到近乎像样的数目，那一下震动仍是他始料未及。他思忖，拿这件事去论证奇迹实有其事，倒是个现成的好例证。

===Original===
He rose, picking up his trench coat.

===Chinese===
他站起身，拎起风衣。

===Original===
“Well! That’s something like it, now,” he said. “I’ll see you later; I don’t know how long it’s going to take me to get a list prepared, and circularize the old-arms trade. I should hear from everybody who’s interested in a few weeks. You can be sure I’ll keep your offer in mind.”

===Chinese===
「好哇！这才像话，」他说，「回头见；编一份货单、再向古董枪这行当广发通函，得花多久我说不准。过几个星期，凡有兴趣的都该给我回音了。您的报价，我保管记在心上。」

===Original===
He slipped into the coat and put on his hat, and then picked up the package containing the Confederate revolver. Rivers had risen, too; he was watching Rand nervously. When Rand tucked the package under his arm and began drawing on his gloves, Rivers cleared his throat.

===Chinese===
他套上风衣，戴好帽子，随后拿起包着那支邦联转轮枪的纸包。里弗斯也站了起来，紧张地望着兰德。待兰德把纸包夹上腋下、动手戴手套，里弗斯清了清嗓子。

===Original===
“Mr. Rand, I’m dreadfully sorry,” he began, “but I’ll have to return your money and take back that revolver. It should not have been sold.” He got Rand’s sixty dollars out of his pocket as though he expected it to catch fire, and held it out.

===Chinese===
「兰德先生，实在对不住，」他开口道，「可我不得不退您的钱，收回那支转轮枪。它本不该卖的。」他从口袋里掏出兰德那六十美元，那急切劲儿，像防着它在兜里烧起来似的，把钱递了过来。

===Original===
Rand favored him with a display of pained surprise.

===Chinese===
兰德回敬他一副痛心惊讶的神色。

===Original===
“Why, I can’t do that,” he replied. “I bought this revolver in good faith, and you accepted payment and were satisfied with the transaction. The sale’s been made, now.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，这可办不到，」他答道，「我是诚心诚意买下这支转轮枪的，您也收了钱，对这桩买卖表示满意。如今买卖已经做成了。」

===Original===
Rivers seemed distressed. It was probably the first time he had ever been on the receiving end of that routine, and he didn’t like it.

===Chinese===
里弗斯显得很苦恼。这套把戏向来只有他施与人的份儿，落到自己头上领受，恐怕还是头一遭，他很不痛快。

===Original===
“Now you’re being unreasonable, Mr. Rand,” he protested. “Look here; I’ll give you seventy-five dollars’ credit on anything else in the shop. You certainly can’t find fault with an offer like that.”

===Chinese===
「您这就蛮不讲理了，兰德先生，」他抗议道，「您瞧；店里随便哪件别的东西，我给您七十五美元的记账额。这样的提议，您总归挑不出毛病了吧。」

===Original===
“I don’t want anything else in the shop; I want this revolver you sold me.” Rand gave him a look of supercilious insolence that was at least a two hundred percent improvement on Rivers at his most insolent. “You know, I’ll begin to acquire a poor idea of your business methods before long,” he added.

===Chinese===
「店里的别的东西我一样不要；我要的就是您卖我的这支转轮枪。」兰德投去一记目中无人的眼色，那份倨傲，比里弗斯最无礼的时候还要高出至少两倍。「您知道吗，要不了多久，我就要对您的经商之道形成很坏的观感了，」他补了一句。

===Original===
Rivers laughed ruefully. “Well, to tell the truth, I just remembered a customer of mine who specializes in Confederate arms, who would pay me at least eighty for that item,” he admitted. “I thought﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
里弗斯苦笑。「唉，说实在的，我刚想起我有位专收邦联枪械的主顾，这一件他少说肯出我八十，」他承认道，「我寻思……」

===Original===
Rand shook his head. “I have a special fondness for Confederate arms, myself. One of my grandfathers was in Mosby’s Rangers, and the other was with Barksdale, to say nothing of about a dozen great-uncles and so on.”

===Chinese===
兰德摇头。「我自己对邦联枪械就别有钟情。我一位祖父当年在莫斯比的游骑兵（Mosby's Rangers）里服役，另一位在巴克斯代尔（Barksdale）麾下作战，更不消说还有十来位叔伯祖父之辈。」

===Original===
“Well, you’re entirely within your rights, Mr. Rand,” Rivers conceded. “I should apologize for trying to renege on a sale, but.﻿ ﻿… Well, I hope to see you again, soon.” He followed Rand to the door, shaking hands with him. “Don’t forget; I’m willing to pay anything up to twenty-five thousand for the Fleming collection.”

===Chinese===
「好吧，您完全在理，兰德先生，」里弗斯让了步，「想赖掉一桩卖定的买卖，我本该道歉，可是。……好吧，盼望不久再见到您。」他跟着兰德走到门口，同他握手。「别忘了；弗莱明这批收藏，两万五以内，什么价我都肯出。」

## VI / 六

===Original===
The Fleming butler—Walters, Rand remembered Gladys Fleming having called him—became apologetic upon learning who the visitor was.

===Chinese===
弗莱明家的管家——沃尔特斯（Walters），兰德记得格拉迪斯·弗莱明是这么称呼他的——一得悉来客是谁，便忙不迭赔起不是来。

===Original===
“Forgive me, Colonel Rand, but I’m afraid I must put you to some inconvenience, sir,” he said. “You see, we have no chauffeur, at present, and I don’t drive very well, myself. Would you object to putting up your own car, sir? The garage is under the house, at the rear; just follow the driveway around. I’ll go through the house and meet you there for the luggage. I’m dreadfully sorry to put you to the trouble, but. …”

===Chinese===
「恕罪，兰德上校，只怕要给您添些不便了，先生，」他说，「您瞧，眼下我们没雇司机，我自己车又开得不济。您自己把车停了，先生，可会介意？车库在宅子底下，靠后头；顺着车道绕过去便是。我这就穿宅子过去，到那儿帮您取行李。实在抱歉劳您的驾，可是。……」

===Original===
“Oh, that’s all right,” Rand comforted him. “Just as soon do it, myself, now, anyhow. I expect to be in and out with the car while I’m here, and I’d better learn the layout of the garage now.”

===Chinese===
「哦，不妨事，」兰德安慰他，「横竖如今我自己来还更乐意些。我在这儿住着，进进出出少不了用车，不如趁现在先摸熟车库的格局。」

===Original===
“You may back in, sir, or drive straight in and back out,” the butler told him. “One way’s about as easy as the other.”

===Chinese===
「您可以倒着开进去，先生，也可以直着开进去、回头再倒出来，」管家告诉他，「两个法子难易差不多。」

===Original===
Rand returned to his car, driving around the house. A row of doors opened out of the basement garage; Walters, who must have gone through the house on the double, was waiting for him. Having what amounted to a conditioned reflex to park his car so that he could get it out as fast as possible, he cut over to the right, jockeyed a little, and backed in. There were already two cars in the garage; a big maroon Packard sedan, and a sand-colored Packard station-wagon, standing side by side. Rand put his Lincoln in on the left of the sedan.

===Chinese===
兰德回到自己车上，绕着宅子开过去。地下车库开出一排库门；沃尔特斯——他准是一路小跑穿宅而过——已在等他。把车停成随时能最快驶出的姿态，于他近乎一种条件反射；他向右一拐，前后腾挪了两把，倒车入位。车库里已停着两辆车：一辆枣红色帕卡德（Packard）大轿车，一辆沙色的帕卡德旅行车，并排而立。兰德把林肯（Lincoln）停在轿车左侧。

===Original===
“Bags in the luggage-compartment; it isn’t locked,” he told the butler, making sure that the glove-compartment, where he had placed the Leech & Rigdon revolver, was locked. As he got out, the servant went to the rear of the car and took out the Gladstone and the B-4 bag Rand had brought with him.

===Chinese===
「行李在后备厢里；没上锁，」他告诉管家，同时确认杂物箱已锁好——那支利奇与里格登转轮枪就搁在里头。他下车时，仆人绕到车尾，取出兰德随身带来的格拉德斯通（Gladstone）手提包和 B-4 行囊。

===Original===
“If you don’t mind entering the house from the rear, sir, we can go up those steps, there, and through the rear hall,” the butler suggested, almost as though he were making some indecent and criminal proposal.

===Chinese===
「您若不介意从后头进宅，先生，咱们可以上那边的台阶，穿后厅进去，」管家提议道，那副神情，倒像在提什么有伤风化的犯罪勾当。

===Original===
Rand told him to forget the protocol and lead the way. The butler picked up the bags and conducted him up a short flight of concrete steps to a landing and a door opening into a short hall above. An open door from this gave access to a longer hall, stretching to the front of the house, and there was a third door, closed, which probably led to the servants’ domain.

===Chinese===
兰德叫他把繁文缛节丢开，只管前头带路。管家提起行李，引他上一小段水泥台阶，到一方平台，一扇门通进上面一条短过道。短过道边一扇敞开的门，又通向一条更长的过道，直抵宅子前部；还有第三扇门关着，多半通往仆役的地界。

===Original===
Rand followed his guide through the open door and into the long hall, which passed under an arch to extend to the front door. There was a door on either side, about midway to the arch under the front stairway; the one on the right was the dining-room, Walters explained, and the one on the left was the library. He seemed to be still suffering from the ignominy of admitting a house-guest through any but the main portal.

===Chinese===
兰德随着引路人穿过那扇敞开的门，进了长过道；过道经一道拱门底下，一直延伸到正门。离拱门约莫一半路的地方——前楼梯正从那拱门上方跨过——两侧各有一扇门；沃尔特斯解释说，右边是餐厅，左边是书房。他似乎还在为让留宿的客人走旁门而非正门而煎熬着一份羞耻。

===Original===
Emerging into the front hallway, he put down the bags, took Rand’s hat and coat and laid them on top of the luggage, and then went to an open doorway on the right, standing in it and coughing delicately, before announcing that Colonel Rand was here.

===Chinese===
到了前厅，他放下行李，接过兰德的帽子和外套搭在行李上头，然后走到右边一个敞开的门洞里，站在那儿文雅地轻咳一声，才通禀兰德上校到了。

===Original===
Gladys Fleming, wearing a pale blue frock, came forward as Rand entered the parlor, her hand extended. The two other women in the big parlor remained motionless. They would be the sisters, Geraldine Varcek and Nelda Dunmore. Rand didn’t wonder that they resented Gladys so bitterly; economic considerations aside, girls seldom enthuse over a stepmother so near their own age who is so much more beautiful.

===Chinese===
兰德走进客厅时，格拉迪斯·弗莱明身穿一袭淡青色连衣裙迎上前来，伸出手。大客厅里另外两位女宾纹丝不动。想必就是那对姐妹：杰拉尔丁·瓦尔切克和内尔达·邓莫尔。她们对格拉迪斯怨毒至此，兰德并不奇怪；经济账且撇开不谈，姑娘们难得对一位年纪与己相仿、又美貌远胜于己的继母热络得起来。

===Original===
“Good afternoon, Colonel Rand,” Gladys said. “This is Mrs. Varcek.” She indicated a very pale blonde who sat slumped in a deep chair beside a low cocktail-table, a highball in her hand. “And Mrs. Dunmore.” She was the brunette with the full bust and hips, in the short black skirt and the tight white sweater, who was standing by the fireplace.

===Chinese===
「下午好，兰德上校，」格拉迪斯说，「这位是瓦尔切克太太。」她指向一位面色白得没有血色的金发女子，她瘫坐在矮鸡尾酒桌旁一张深椅里，手里端着一杯高杯酒。「这位是邓莫尔太太。」那是位深色头发、深色皮肤的美人，胸脯丰腴、胯骨宽厚，一身黑色短裙配紧身的白毛衣，正站在壁炉边。

===Original===
“H’lo.” The blonde—Geraldine—smiled shyly at him. She had big blue eyes, and delicately tinted rose-petal lips that seemed to be trying not to laugh at some private joke. She wasn’t exactly blotto, but she had evidently laid a good foundation for a first-class jag. After all, it was only two thirty in the afternoon.

===Chinese===
「哈罗。」那位金发女子——杰拉尔丁——朝他羞怯怯一笑。她有一双大大的蓝眼睛，两瓣着色浅淡、玫瑰花瓣似的嘴唇，瞧着像在憋着一个只有她自己知道的笑话，怎么也不肯笑出来。她还谈不上烂醉，但显然已为一场头等的豪饮打好了底子。毕竟，此刻不过是下午两点半。

===Original===
The other sister—Nelda—didn’t say anything. She merely stood and stared at Rand distrustfully. Rand doubted that she ordinarily gave men the hostile eye. The full, dark-red lips; the lush figure; the way she draped it against the side of the fireplace, to catch the ruddy light on her more interesting curves and bulges—there was a bimbo just made to be leered at, and she probably resented it like hell if she weren’t.

===Chinese===
另一位姐妹——内尔达——一言不发，只是站在那儿，满腹狐疑地瞪着兰德。兰德不信她平日也这样敌视男人。那两瓣丰润的深红嘴唇；那副丰腴的身段；她把身子斜倚在壁炉侧边的姿态，好让炉火的红光恰照在她身上更有看头的曲线上——这么一个女人，生来就是给人色迷迷盯看的；倘若真没人来盯，她才会恼火得要死。

===Original===
Rand gave them a general good afternoon, then turned to Gladys. “I had a talk with Goode, yesterday afternoon,” he said. “I have his authorization to handle all the details. As soon as I get an itemized list, I’ll circularize dealers and other possible buyers and ask for offers.”

===Chinese===
兰德朝她们笼统道了声下午好，便转向格拉迪斯。「昨天下午我和古德谈过了，」他说，「我有他的委托，经办全部细节。一俟拿到逐件开列的清单，我就向各古董商和其他可能的买家广发通函，征求报价。」

===Original===
“Is that all?” Nelda demanded angrily of Gladys. “Why Fred’s done all that already!”

===Chinese===
「就这些？」内尔达怒气冲冲地质问格拉迪斯，「这些弗雷德不早就全办完了嘛！」

===Original===
“Is that correct, Mrs. Fleming?” Rand asked, for the record.

===Chinese===
「属实吗，弗莱明太太？」兰德问了一句，算是立此存照。

===Original===
“I told you, yesterday, what’s been done,” Gladys replied. “Fred has talked to one dealer, Arnold Rivers. There has been no inventory of any sort made.”

===Chinese===
「昨天我就告诉过你办到了哪一步，」格拉迪斯答道，「弗雷德只跟一个商人谈过，阿诺德·里弗斯。任何形式的清点编目都不曾做过。」

===Original===
“Mr. Rivers is offering us ten thousand dollars,” Nelda retorted. “I don’t see why you had to bring this Colonel What’s-his-name into it, at all. You think he can get us a better offer? If you do, you’re crazy!”

===Chinese===
「里弗斯先生可是出价一万美元要买，」内尔达反唇相讥，「我压根儿不明白，你何必把这位叫什么名字的上校拉进来。你以为他能替我们多卖几个钱？你要真这么想，就是疯了！」

===Original===
“Ten thousand dollars, for a collection that ought to sell for five times that, in Macy’s basement!” Geraldine hooted. “How much is Rivers slipping Fred, on the side?”

===Chinese===
「一万块！这么一批收藏，照理该卖五倍的价——这价钱也就梅西（Macy’s）百货的地下贱卖场见得着！」杰拉尔丁嗤笑出声，「里弗斯私底下塞给弗雷德多少？」

===Original===
“Oh, go back to your bottle!” Nelda cried. “You’re too drunk to know what you’re talking about!”

===Chinese===
「哦，回你那酒瓶子边上去！」内尔达嚷道，「你醉得都不知自己在胡吣什么了！」

===Original===
“They tell me Colonel Rand is a detective, too,” Geraldine continued. “Maybe he can find out why Fred never talked to Stephen Gresham, or Carl Gwinnett, or anybody else except this Rivers. How much *is* Fred getting out of Rivers, anyhow?”

===Chinese===
「听说这位兰德上校还是个侦探呢，」杰拉尔丁自顾自说下去，「兴许他能查出，弗雷德干吗从来没找过斯蒂芬·格雷沙姆，没找过卡尔·格温内特（Carl Gwinnett），也没找过旁的什么人，光找这个里弗斯。弗雷德到底从里弗斯手里落了多少好处？」

===Original===
“My God, Geraldine, shut up!” Nelda howled. Then she decided to take direct notice of Rand’s presence. “Colonel Rand, I’m sorry to say that, in her present condition, my sister doesn’t know what she’s saying. It’s bad enough for my stepmother to bring an outsider into what’s obviously a family matter, but when my sister begins making these ridiculous accusations. …”

===Chinese===
「天哪，杰拉尔丁，闭嘴！」内尔达吼道。随即她决定正面理会兰德的存在。「兰德上校，很遗憾，我妹妹眼下这副状态，自己说的是什么都不知道。我继母把外人拉进一桩明摆着的家务事，已经够不像话了；偏我妹妹又编排起这些荒唐的指控……」

===Original===
“What’s ridiculous about them?” Geraldine demanded, dumping another two ounces of whiskey into her glass and freshening it with the siphon. “I think Rivers’s offering ten thousand dollars for the collection, and Fred’s thinking we’d accept it, are the only ridiculous things about it.”

===Chinese===
「荒唐在哪儿？」杰拉尔丁质问道，又往杯里倒了两盎司威士忌，用苏打水虹吸瓶兑了兑。「依我说，里弗斯出这一万块买这批收藏，弗雷德还当我们会点头，这才是天底下仅有的两桩荒唐事。」

===Original===
“That’s rather what I told Rivers, this afternoon,” Rand put in. “He seemed a bit upset about my being brought into this, too, but he finally admitted that he was willing to pay up to twenty-five thousand dollars for the collection, and if he buys it, that’s exactly what it’s going to cost him.”

===Chinese===
「今天下午我跟里弗斯说的，大致也是这个意思，」兰德插话道，「他对我被请来插手这事，似乎也颇有几分不忿，但末了还是承认，这批收藏他肯出到两万五；他若真买下，这批货就得让他破费到这个数。」

===Original===
“*What?*” Nelda fairly screamed. Her hands opened and closed spasmodically: she was using a dark-red nail-tint that made Rand think of blood-dripping talons.

===Chinese===
「什么？」内尔达简直是尖叫了。她的手一阵阵痉挛似的张开又攥紧：她涂着深红色的指甲油，那颜色让兰德想起滴血的利爪。

===Original===
“Mr. Arnold Rivers told me, this afternoon, and I quote: I’m willing to pay up to twenty-five thousand dollars for that collection, unquote,” Rand said. “And I can tell you now that twenty-five thousand dollars is just what he will pay for it, unless I can find somebody who’s willing to pay more, which is not at all improbable.”

===Chinese===
「阿诺德·里弗斯先生今天下午亲口告诉我的，我引他的原话：这批收藏，我肯出到两万五千美元，引毕，」兰德说，「而且我现在就可以告诉诸位，两万五正是他将来实打实要付的数——除非我能找到肯出更高价的买主，而这绝不是不可能的事。」

===Original===
“H’ray!” Geraldine waved her glass and toasted Rand with it. “And twenty-five G ain’t hay, brother!”

===Chinese===
「好哇！」杰拉尔丁挥着酒杯，朝兰德敬了一下，「两万五可不是仨瓜俩枣，老兄！」

===Original===
Gladys smiled quickly at Rand, then turned to Nelda. “Now I hope you see why I thought it wise to bring in somebody who knows something about old arms,” she said.

===Chinese===
格拉迪斯朝兰德飞快一笑，随即转向内尔达。「这下你该明白，我为什么觉得请一位懂古董枪的人来是明智之举了，」她说。

===Original===
Nelda evidently saw; there was apparently nothing stupid about her. “And Fred was going to take a miserable ten thousand dollars!” The way she said it, ten thousand sounded like a fairly generous headwaiter’s tip. “Did Rivers actually tell you he’d pay twenty-five?”

===Chinese===
内尔达显然明白了；她这人显然一点都不蠢。「而弗雷德居然打算收下可怜巴巴的一万块！」照她那口气，一万美元听着倒像一笔出手相当阔绰的领班小费。「里弗斯真是亲口跟你说，肯付两万五？」

===Original===
Rand gave, as nearly verbatim as possible, his conversation with the dealer. “And he can afford it, too,” he finished. “He can make a nice profit on the collection, at that figure.”

===Chinese===
兰德把同那位古董商的谈话，尽量一字不差地复述了一遍。「而且这个价他也出得起，」他末了说，「照这个数吃下这批收藏，他还有得一番好赚头。」

===Original===
“My God, do you mean the pistols are worth more than that, even?” she wanted to know, aghast.

===Chinese===
「天哪，你是说这些手枪的身价竟还不止这个数？」她惊骇不定，非要问个明白。

===Original===
“Certainly, if you’re a dealer with an established business, and customers all over the country, and want to take five or six years to make your profit,” Rand replied. “If you aren’t, and want your money in a hurry, no.”

===Chinese===
「当然是，」兰德答道，「前提你是位根基稳固的商人，主顾遍及全国，又肯花上五六年工夫慢慢图利。若不是这样的人，又急着拿钱——那就不值。」

===Original===
“That’s why I was against turning the collection over to Gwinnett on a commission basis,” Gladys said. “It would take him five years to get everything sold.”

===Chinese===
「所以我才反对把这批收藏放到格温内特那儿寄售，」格拉迪斯说，「叫他经手，五年也出不完这批货。」

===Original===
Nelda left the fireplace and advanced toward Rand. “Colonel, I owe you an apology,” she said. “I had no idea Father’s pistols were worth anywhere near that much. I don’t suppose Fred did, either.” She frowned. Wait till she gets Fred alone, Rand thought; I’d hate to be in his spot. … “You say you’re acting on Humphrey Goode’s authority?”

===Chinese===
内尔达离开壁炉，向兰德走近几步。「上校，我该向您赔个不是，」她说，「我全然不知父亲的这些手枪竟值这么多。我想弗雷德也未必知道。」她皱起眉头。等她寻着机会单独对付弗雷德吧，兰德心想；我可不愿站他那个位置。……「您说，您是奉汉弗莱·古德之命行事？」

===Original===
“That’s right. I’ll negotiate the sale, but the money will be paid directly to him, for distribution according to the terms of your father’s will.” Rand got out Goode’s letter and handed it to Nelda.

===Chinese===
「正是。由我来议定出售，但货款将直接付给他，再按令尊遗嘱的条款分配。」兰德取出古德那封信，递给内尔达。

===Original===
She read it carefully. “I see.” She seemed greatly relieved; she was looking at Rand, now, as she was accustomed to look at men, particularly handsome six-footers who were broad across the shoulders and narrow at the hips and resembled King Charles II. She was probably wondering if Rand was equal to Old Rowley in another important respect. “I didn’t understand… I thought. …” A dirty look, aimed at Gladys, explained what she had thought. Then her glance fell on the bottle and siphon on the table beside Geraldine’s chair, and she changed the subject by inquiring if Colonel Rand mightn’t like a drink.

===Chinese===
她仔细读过。「明白了。」她像是大大松了口气；此刻她打量兰德的目光，已换上了她惯于看男人的那种，尤其对着英俊的六英尺汉子——肩宽、胯窄、生得像查理二世（King Charles II）国王的那路人。她多半正在琢磨，在另一桩要紧本事上，兰德比不比得上「老罗利」（查理二世的诨名）。「我先前不明白……我还当……」她朝格拉迪斯剜过去的一记眼刀，道破了她当过什么。随后她的目光落到杰拉尔丁椅旁桌上的酒瓶和苏打水虹吸瓶上，便换了个话头，问兰德上校可愿来一杯。

===Original===
“Well, let’s go up to the gunroom,” Gladys suggested. “We can have our drink up there, while Colonel Rand’s looking at the pistols. … Coming with us, Geraldine?”

===Chinese===
「那么，咱们上枪房去吧，」格拉迪斯提议，「酒就带到上头喝，正好兰德上校看枪。……你也来吗，杰拉尔丁？」

===Original===
Geraldine rose, not too steadily, her glass still in her hand, and took Rand’s left arm. Gladys, seeing Nelda moving in on the detective’s right, took his other arm. Nelda was barely successful in suppressing a look of murderous anger. The double doorway into the hall was just wide enough for Rand and his two flankers to pass through; Nelda had to fall in a couple of paces rear of center, and wasn’t able to come up into line until they were in the hall upstairs.

===Chinese===
杰拉尔丁起身，站得不算太稳，手里仍端着酒杯，挽住了兰德的左臂。格拉迪斯眼见内尔达向这位侦探的右翼包抄过去，便占住了他另一条胳膊。内尔达好不容易才压下脸上那副杀气腾腾的神色。通往过道的双开门，宽度刚够兰德和他左右两翼的护卫并肩通过；内尔达只得缀在队形中腰之后两步，直到上了楼进了过道，才得着机会凑上前来。

===Original===
“There’s the gunroom.” Gladys pointed. “And that’s your room, over there.” As she spoke, Walters came out of the doorway she had indicated.

===Chinese===
「那就是枪房。」格拉迪斯一指。「那边那间是你的屋子。」话音未落，沃尔特斯正从她指的那扇门里出来。

===Original===
“Your bags are unpacked, sir,” he reported. Then he told Rand where he would find his things, and where the bath was.

===Chinese===
「您的行李都拆放好了，先生，」他禀报。接着告诉兰德东西搁在哪儿，浴室在哪儿。

===Original===
There was a brief discussion of drinks. The butler received his instructions and went down the stairway; Rand broke up the feminine formation around him and ushered the ladies ahead of him into the gunroom.

===Chinese===
关于酒水略作了一番商议。管家领了吩咐，下楼去了；兰德解散了环绕自己身边的娘儿们编队，请两位女士先行，进了枪房。

===Original===
It was much as he remembered it from his visit of two years before. There was a desk in one corner, and back of it a short workbench and tool-cabinet. There was a long table in the middle of the room, its top covered with green baize, upon which many flat rectangular boxes of hardwood rested—some walnut, some rosewood, some quartered oak. Each would contain a pistol or pair of pistols, with cleaning and loading tools. In the corner farthest from the desk, he saw the head of the spiral stairway from the library below, mentioned by Gladys Fleming. There were ashstands and a couple of cocktail-tables, and a number of chairs, and the old maple cobbler’s bench on which Lane Fleming had died. The only books in the room were in a small case over the workbench; they were all arms-books.

===Chinese===
屋里的情形，与他两年前造访时的记忆大体不差。一角放着一张写字台，台后是一张短短的工作台和工具柜。屋子正中一张长桌，桌面覆着绿呢，上面搁着许多扁平的硬木匣——有的胡桃木，有的花梨木，有的径切橡木。每只匣子里想必躺着一支或一对枪，随匣还配着保养和装填的工具。离写字台最远的那个角落，他看见了自楼下书房上来的螺旋楼梯口——格拉迪斯·弗莱明提起过它。屋里有几座烟灰立架、两张鸡尾酒桌、若干把椅子，还有那张旧枫木鞋匠工作台——莱恩·弗莱明就是毙命在它上面的。屋里仅有的书，放在工作台上方一只小书柜里，全是兵器书。

===Original===
Then he looked at the walls. On both ends, and on the long inside wall, the pistols hung, hundreds and hundreds of them, the cream of a lifetime’s collecting. Horizontal white-painted boards had been fixed to the walls about four feet from the floor, and similar boards had been placed five feet above them. Between, narrow vertical strips, as wide as a lath but twice as thick, were set. Rows of pistols were hung, the barrels horizontal, on pairs of these strips, with screwhooks at grip and muzzle. There were about a hundred such vertical rows of pistols.

===Chinese===
然后他望向墙壁。两端的山墙和内侧的长墙上，手枪挂得满满当当，数以百计，是一位藏家毕生收藏的精华。离地约四英尺处，墙上钉着漆成白色的水平木板，上方五英尺处又钉了同样的板。两板之间，竖着一条条窄木条，宽如灰板条，厚却倍之。一排排手枪就挂在这些成对的竖条上，枪管放平，握把与枪口处各有一只螺丝钩承托。这样的竖排手枪，约有百列之数。

===Original===
Rand was still looking at them when the butler brought in the drinks; when Gladys told the servant that that would be all, he went out, rather reluctantly, by the spiral stairs to the library.

===Chinese===
兰德还在端详间，管家把酒水送了进来；格拉迪斯对仆人说声没有别的事了，他便从通往书房的螺旋楼梯退出去了，走得不甚情愿。

===Original===
“Well, what do you think of them, Colonel Rand?” Gladys asked.

===Chinese===
「怎么样，兰德上校，您觉得这批枪如何？」格拉迪斯问。

===Original===
Rand tasted his whiskey and looked around. “It’s one of the finest collections in the country,” he said. “I may even be able to find somebody who’ll top Rivers’s offer, but don’t be disappointed if I don’t. … By the way, did anybody help Mr. Fleming keep this stuff clean? The room seems dry, but even so, they’d need an occasional wiping-off.”

===Chinese===
兰德抿了一口威士忌，环顾四周。「这是全国数得着的收藏之一，」他说，「我说不定还能找到出价压过里弗斯的主顾，但若找不到，也请莫失望。……对了，弗莱明先生平日可有人帮着保养这些物件？这屋子看着是干燥，即便如此，它们隔三差五也该擦拭一回。」

===Original===
“Oh, Walters was always in here, going over the pistols,” Nelda said. “He’s been in here every day, lately.”

===Chinese===
「哦，沃尔特斯总在这屋里检视保养这些枪，」内尔达说，「近来他天天都来。」

===Original===
“I wonder if you could spare him to help me a little? I’ll need somebody who knows his way around here, at first.”

===Chinese===
「能不能把他借我帮帮忙？起先我总得有个熟门熟路的人。」

===Original===
“Why, of course,” Gladys agreed. “He isn’t very busy in the mornings, or in the afternoons till close to dinnertime. Are you going to start work today?”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，当然可以，」格拉迪斯应道，「他上午不很忙，下午不到挨近晚饭的时辰也闲着。您今天就动手吗？」

===Original===
“I’ll have to. I’m going to see Stephen Gresham and his associates this evening, and I’ll want to know what I’m talking about.”

===Chinese===
「不得不如此。今晚我就要去见斯蒂芬·格雷沙姆和他那几位同道，总得先弄明白自己要谈的是什么。」

===Original===
They spent about fifteen minutes over their drinks, talking about the collection. Rand and Gladys did most of the talking, in spite of Nelda’s best efforts to monopolize the conversation. Geraldine, after a few minutes, retired into her private world and only roused herself when her sister and stepmother were about to leave. When they went out, Gladys promised to send Walters up directly; Rand heard her speaking to him at the foot of the main stairway.

===Chinese===
三人就着酒谈了约莫一刻钟的收藏。尽管内尔达使出浑身解数要独揽话头，说话的主要还是兰德和格拉迪斯。杰拉尔丁搭了几分钟话，便退回她的私人世界里去了，直到她妹妹和继母将要离开时才回过神来。两人出门时，格拉迪斯答应即刻打发沃尔特斯上来；兰德听见她在主楼梯脚下同他说话。

## VII / 七

===Original===
When Walters entered, Rand had his pipe lit and was walking slowly around the room, laying out the work ahead of him. Roughly, the earliest pieces were on the extreme left, on the short north wall of the room, and the most recent ones on the right, at the south end. This was, of course, only relatively true; the pistols seemed to have been classified by type in vertical rows, and chronologically from top to bottom in each row. The collection seemed to consist of a number of intensely specialized small groups, with a large number of pistols of general types added. For instance, about midway on the long east wall, there were some thirty-odd all-metal pistols, from wheel lock to percussion. There was a collection of U.S. Martials, with two rows of the regulation pistols, flintlock and percussion, of foreign governments, placed on the left, and the collection of Colts on the right. After them came the other types of percussion revolvers, and the later metallic-cartridge types.

===Chinese===
沃尔特斯进来时，兰德已点着了烟斗，正绕着屋子缓缓踱步，盘算面前的活计。大体说来，年代最早的枪挂在最左端，即屋子北侧的短墙上；年代最近的挂在右边，南端。当然，这说法只是相对成立；这些手枪似乎按类型分列成一条条竖排，每排之内再依年代自上而下。整批收藏似乎由若干极为专门的小组构成，另添了大量泛常类型的枪。比方说，东侧长墙约居中的地方，挂着三十来支全金属手枪，从转轮打火枪到火帽击发枪都有。还有一组美国军用型（U.S. Martial）收藏，左边另挂两排各国政府的制式手枪——燧发与火帽击发皆有——右边则是柯尔特（Colt）枪的收藏。柯尔特之后，是其他类型的火帽击发转轮枪，以及更晚的金属弹枪型。

===Original===
It was an arrangement which made sense, from the arms student's point of view, and Rand decided that it would make sense to the dealers and museums to whom he intended sending lists. He would save time by listing them as they were hung on the walls. Then, there were the cases between the windows on the west wall, containing the ammunition collection—examples of every type of fixed-pistol ammunition—and the collection of bullet-molds and powder flasks and wheel lock spanners and assorted cleaning and loading accessories. All that stuff would have to be listed, too.

===Chinese===
从兵器研究者的角度看，这样的布置颇为在理；兰德断定，对他打算寄送清单去的那些古董商和博物馆来说，同样一目了然。照枪挂上墙的次序开单，可以省些工夫。此外，西墙两窗之间还有几口陈列柜，装着弹药收藏——各式手枪定装弹的标本——还有弹丸铸模、火药瓶、转轮打火枪的上弦扳手，以及形形色色的保养装填附件。这些物件也须一一开列。

===Original===
"I beg your pardon, sir," Walters broke in, behind him. "Mrs. Fleming said that you wanted me."

===Chinese===
「恕我打扰，先生，」沃尔特斯在他身后插话道，「弗莱明太太说您找我。」

===Original===
"Oh, yes." Rand turned. "Is this the whole thing? What's on the walls, here?"

===Chinese===
「哦，正是。」兰德转过身。「全都在这儿了？就是这墙上挂的这些？」

===Original===
"Yes, sir. There is also a wall-case containing a number of modern pistols and revolvers, and several rifles and shotguns, in the room formerly occupied by Mr. Fleming, but they are not part of the collection, and they are now the personal property of Mrs. Fleming. I understand that she intends selling at least some of them, on her own account. Then, there is a quantity of ammunition and ammunition-components in that closet under the workbench—cartridges, primed cartridge-shells, black and smokeless powder, cartridge-primers, percussion caps—but they are not part of the collection, either. I believe Mrs. Fleming wants to sell most of that, too."

===Chinese===
「是的，先生。弗莱明先生原先住的那间屋里还有一口壁柜，里面放着若干现代手枪和转轮枪，另有几支步枪和霰弹枪，但它们不在收藏之列，如今是弗莱明太太的私产。据我所知，她打算至少卖掉其中一部分，自己作主出手。再有，工作台底下那口柜橱里存着一批弹药和弹药元件——成弹、装好底火的空弹壳、黑火药与无烟火药、枪弹底火、火帽——但它们同样不算收藏。我想这批东西的大半，弗莱明太太也是要卖的。」

===Original===
"Well, I'll talk to her about it. I may want to buy some of the ammunition for myself," Rand said. "So I only need to bother with what's on the walls, in this room? … By the way, did Mr. Fleming keep any sort of record of his collection? A book, or a card-index, or anything like that?"

===Chinese===
「好，这事我跟她谈。那批弹药里兴许有我自己想买的，」兰德说，「这么说，我要操心的就只有这间屋墙上挂的了？……对了，弗莱明先生给他的收藏留下过什么账目吗？簿子，或者卡片索引，诸如此类？」

===Original===
"Why no, sir." Walters was positive. Then he hedged. "If he did, I never saw or heard of anything of the sort. Mr. Fleming knew everything in this room. I've seen him, downstairs, when somebody would ask him about something, close his eyes as though trying to visualize and then give a perfect description of any pistol in the collection. Or else, he could enumerate all the pistols of a certain type; say, all the Philadelphia Deringers, or all the Allen pepperboxes, or all the rim-fire Smith & Wesson tip-back types. He had a remarkable memory for his pistols, although it was not out of the ordinary otherwise, sir."

===Chinese===
「哎呀，没有，先生。」沃尔特斯说得斩钉截铁，随即又留了余地。「就算有，我也从没见过、没听说过这样的东西。这屋里的一切，弗莱明先生了如指掌。我见过他在楼下，有人问起某支枪时，他闭上眼睛，像在心里过画儿似的，然后把这批收藏里随便哪支手枪描述得纤毫不爽。再不然，他能把某一类型的枪一支支尽数报来；比如说，所有的费城德林杰（Philadelphia Deringer），所有的阿伦（Allen）胡椒盒枪，或者所有的史密斯-韦森（Smith & Wesson）边缘发火上折式。对收藏的枪，他的记性惊人，虽然在别的事上倒也平常，先生。」

===Original===
Rand nodded. Any collector—at least, any collector who was a serious arms-student—could do that, particularly if he were a good visualizer and kept his stuff in some systematic order. At the moment, he could have named and described any or all of his own modest collection of two hundred-odd pistols and revolvers.

===Chinese===
兰德点头。任何藏家——至少，任何当真的兵器研究者——都做得到这一步，尤其是擅长形象记忆、又把藏品按某种系统摆得井井有条的人。此时此刻，他自己那区区两百来支手枪与转轮枪的收藏，无论单指一支还是尽数指来，他都能说出名目、描述周详。

===Original===
"I was hoping he'd kept a record," he said. "A great many collectors do, and it would have helped me quite a bit." He made up his mind to compile such a record, himself, when he got back to New Belfast. It would be a big help to Carter Tipton, when it came time to settle his own estate, and a man on whom the Reaper has scored as many near-misses as on Jeff Rand should begin to think of such things. "And how about writing materials? And is there a typewriter available?"

===Chinese===
「我还指望他留了账目呢，」他说，「许许多多藏家都有这个习惯，那于我可就帮大忙了。」他打定主意，回到新贝尔法斯特（New Belfast）后，自己也编一份这样的账。将来清算他自己的遗产时，这对卡特·蒂普顿（Carter Tipton）会是大大的助力——一个像杰夫·兰德这样被死神记下过那么多次擦身而过的人，也该开始想想这些事了。「再有，书写文具呢？有打字机可用吗？」

===Original===
There was: a cased portable was on the floor beside the workbench. Walters showed him which desk drawers contained paper and other things. There was, Rand noticed, a loaded .38 Colt Detective Special, in the upper right-hand desk drawer.

===Chinese===
有：工作台旁的地板上立着一台带匣的手提打字机。沃尔特斯指给他看，写字台哪些抽屉里放着纸张杂物。兰德留意到，写字台右上层的抽屉里，躺着一支上了膛的 .38 口径柯尔特警探特型（Detective Special）转轮枪。

===Original===
"And these phones," the butler continued, indicating them. "This one is a private outside phone; it doesn't connect with any other in the house. The other is an extension. It has a buzzer; the outside phone has a regular bell."

===Chinese===
「还有这几部电话，」管家接着说，抬手一指。「这一部是私人外线电话，与宅里其他话机都不相通。另一部是分机，装着蜂鸣器；外线那部用的是寻常电铃。」

===Original===
Rand thanked him for the information. Then, picking up a notepad and pencil, he started on the left of the collection, meaning to make a general list and rough approximation of value for use in talking to Gresham's friends that evening. Tomorrow he would begin on the detailed list for use in soliciting outside offers.

===Chinese===
兰德谢过他告知这些。随后他拿起便笺簿和铅笔，从收藏的左端着手，打算先开一份大略的清单、估个粗略的价钱，好在当晚同格雷沙姆和他的同道洽谈时派用场。明天再着手细目清单，用来征求外间报价。

===Original===
Twenty-five wheel locks: four heavy South German dags, two singles and a pair; three Saxon pistols, with sharply dropped grips, a pair and one single; five French and Italian sixteenth-century pistols; a pair of small pocket or sash pistols; a pair of French petronels, and an extremely long seventeenth-century Dutch pistol with an ivory-covered stock and a carved ivory Venus-head for a pommel; eight seventeenth-century French, Italian and Flemish pistols. Rand noted them down, and was about to pass on; then he looked sharply at one of them.

===Chinese===
二十五支转轮打火枪：四支沉重的南德达格枪（dag），两支单枪、一对；三支萨克森手枪，握把陡垂，一对另加一支单枪；五支十六世纪的法国、意大利手枪；一对小巧的口袋枪，或者叫腰带枪；一对法国佩特罗内尔（petronel）马枪，另有一支极长的十七世纪荷兰手枪，枪托包着象牙，托尾缀一颗雕成维纳斯头像的象牙圆球；八支十七世纪的法国、意大利、弗拉芒手枪。兰德一一记下，正要移步向前，忽然朝其中一支定睛看去。

===Original===
It was nothing out of the ordinary, as wheel locks go; a long Flemish weapon of about 1640, the type used by the Royalist cavalry in the English Civil War. There were two others almost like it, but this one was in simply appalling condition. The metal was rough with rust, and apparently no attempt had been made to clean it in a couple of centuries. There was a piece cracked out of the fore-end, the ramrod was missing, as was the front ramrod-thimble, both the trigger-guard and the butt-cap were loose, and when Rand touched the wheel, it revolved freely if sluggishly, betraying a broken spring or chain.

===Chinese===
以转轮打火枪而论，这支本无出奇之处；一支约 1640 年间的长身弗拉芒枪，英国内战时保王党骑兵用的那一类。旁边另有两支与它几可乱真，唯独这一支，品相糟得骇人。金属上锈痂嶙峋，看模样两三个世纪不曾有人想法子擦洗过。前托崩缺了一块，通条没了，前通条箍也没了，护圈和托底板俱都松动；兰德一拨打火轮，轮子倒是空转得动，只是滞涩——一望而知是发条或链条断了。

===Original===
The vertical row next to it seemed to be all snaphaunces, but among them Rand saw a pair of Turkish flintlocks. Not even good Turkish flintlocks; a pair of the sort of weapons hastily thrown together by native craftsmen or imported ready-made from Belgium for bazaar sale to gullible tourists. Among the fine examples of seventeenth-century Brescian gunmaking above and below it, these things looked like a pair of Dogpatchers in the Waldorf's Starlight Room. Rand contemplated them with distaste, then shrugged. After all, they might have had some sentimental significance; say souvenirs of a pleasantly remembered trip to the Levant.

===Chinese===
它旁边那一竖排，看上去全是撞簧枪（snaphaunce），可兰德在其中瞥见一对土耳其燧发枪。还算不上像样的土耳其燧发枪；是本地工匠手忙脚乱拼凑出来、或是从比利时成批贩来、专在巴扎集市上卖给轻信游客的那种货色。上上下下皆是十七世纪布雷西亚（Brescia）枪匠手艺的精品，这两件东西夹在其间，活像一对混进华尔道夫（Waldorf）酒店星光厅的多格帕奇（Dogpatch，漫画《莱尔·阿布纳》里的乡巴佬镇）土包子。兰德嫌恶地端详了它们片刻，随即耸了耸肩。毕竟，它们或许有着某种情感上的纪念意义；比如说，是一次记忆愉快的黎凡特（Levant）之游的纪念品。

===Original===
A few rows farther on, among some exceptionally fine flintlocks, all of which predated 1700, he saw one of those big Belgian navy pistols, circa 1800, of the sort once advertised far and wide by a certain old-army-goods dealer for $6.95. This was a particularly repulsive specimen of its breed; grimy with hardened dust and gummed oil, maculated with yellow-surface-rust, the brasswork green with corrosion. It was impossible to shrug off a thing like that. From then on, Rand kept his eyes open for similar incongruities.

===Chinese===
再往前几排，在一批格外精美的燧发枪中间——它们无一晚于 1700 年——他瞧见一支那种比利时海军手枪，约莫 1800 年制，正是当年被某家旧军品商人四处登广告、只卖六美元九毛五的货色。这一支又是同类中格外倒胃口的样板：板结的尘垢和发黏的旧油糊满枪身，遍体黄锈斑斑，铜活锈出一层绿。这样的东西就没法耸耸肩装没看见了。打那以后，兰德便留起神来，专找这类格格不入的货。

===Original===
They weren't hard to find. There was a big army pistol, of Central European origin and in abominable condition, among a row of fine multi-shot flintlocks. Multi-shot … Stephen Gresham had mentioned an Elisha Collier flintlock revolver. It wasn't there. It should be hanging about where this post-Napoleonic German thing was.

===Chinese===
这类货找起来并不费事。一排精美的多管燧发枪里，杵着一支中欧出产的大号军用枪，品相恶劣透顶。多管连发……斯蒂芬·格雷沙姆提起过一支伊莱沙·科利尔（Elisha Collier）燧发转轮枪。它不在。按理，它正该挂在这支拿破仑战后年代德国货的位置上。

===Original===
There was no Hall breechloader, either, but there was a dilapidated old Ketland. There were many such interlopers among the U.S. Martials: an English ounce-ball cavalry pistol, a French 1777 and a French 1773, a couple more $6.95 bargain-counter specials, a miserable altered S. North 1816. Among the Colts, there was some awful junk, including a big Spanish hinge-frame .44 and a Belgian imitation of a Webley R.I.C. Model. There weren't as many Paterson Colts as Gresham had spoken of, and the Whitneyville Walker was absent. It went on like that; about a dozen of the best pistols which Rand remembered having seen from two years ago were gone, and he spotted at least twenty items which the late Lane Fleming wouldn't have hung in his backyard privy, if he'd had one.

===Chinese===
霍尔式后装枪也不见了，倒是多出一支破烂不堪的老凯特兰（Ketland）。美国军用型中间，这路闯进来的杂牌货还多着：一支英制一盎司弹径骑兵手枪，一支法国 1777 型、一支法国 1773 型，又是两支六块九毛五的地摊特价货，还有一支改得面目全非的 S·诺斯（S. North）1816 型。柯尔特枪里，也混着些不堪入目的破烂，包括一支西班牙造大型铰接式 .44 口径枪，和一支比利时仿制的韦伯利（Webley）R.I.C.型。帕特森型柯尔特没有格雷沙姆说的那么多，惠特尼维尔·沃克（Whitneyville Walker）也不见踪影。诸如此类，不一而足：兰德记得两年前见过的好枪，约有十几支没了；他还认出至少二十件货色，是已故的莱恩·弗莱明即便后院真有间茅厕、也断不肯往里挂的。

===Original===
Well, that was to be expected. The way these pistols were arranged, the absence of one from its hooks would have been instantly obvious. So, as the good stuff had moved out, these disreputable changelings had moved in.

===Chinese===
唔，这原在意料之中。这些手枪既是如此排列，哪支从挂钩上缺席，本该一眼即知。于是，好货搬出去的同时，这些上不得台面的偷换货便搬了进来。

===Original===
"You had rather a shocking experience here, in Mr. Fleming's death," Rand said, over his shoulder, to the butler.

===Chinese===
「弗莱明先生死在这屋里，您想必受了不小的惊吓，」兰德回头对管家说。

===Original===
"Oh, yes indeed, sir!" Walters seemed relieved that Rand had broken the silence. "A great loss to all of us, sir. And so unexpected."

===Chinese===
「哦，可不是嘛，先生！」沃尔特斯像是巴不得兰德打破这片沉默。「对我们大家都是天大的损失，先生。而且全然料想不到。」

===Original===
He didn't seem averse to talking about it, and went on at some length. His story closely paralleled that of Gladys Fleming.

===Chinese===
他似乎并不忌讳谈这件事，还絮絮说了好一阵。他讲的事，与格拉迪斯·弗莱明所说的几乎处处吻合。

===Original===
"Mr. Varcek called the doctor immediately," he said. "Then Mr. Dunmore pointed out that the doctor would be obliged to notify either the coroner or the police, so he called Mr. Goode, the family solicitor. That was about twenty minutes after the shot. Mr. Goode arrived directly; he was here in about ten minutes. I must say, sir, I was glad to see him; to tell the truth, I had been afraid that the authorities might claim that Mr. Fleming had shot himself deliberately."

===Chinese===
「瓦尔切克先生立刻去请了医生，」他说，「随后邓莫尔先生指出，医生有责任通报验尸官或者警方，于是他又请了古德先生，家里的律师。那是枪响后约莫二十分钟的事。古德先生马上动身，十来分钟便到了府上。我得说，先生，见着他我是松了口气；说句实话，我一直怕官方会咬定弗莱明先生是存心开枪自戕。」

===Original===
Somebody else doesn't like the smell of that accident, Rand thought. Aloud, he said:

===Chinese===
又有一位闻着那桩「意外」不对味，兰德心想。嘴上他说：

===Original===
"Mr. Goode lives nearby, then, I take it?"

===Chinese===
「这么说，古德先生就住左近？」

===Original===
"Oh, yes, sir. You can see his house from these windows. Over here, sir."

===Chinese===
「哦，是的，先生。从这些窗户就望得见他的宅子。到这边来，先生。」

===Original===
Rand looked out the window. The rain-soaked lawn of the Fleming residence ended about a hundred yards to the west; beyond it, an orchard was beginning to break into leaf, and beyond the orchard and another lawn stood a half-timbered Tudor-style house, somewhat smaller than the Fleming place. A path led down from it to the orchard, and another led from the orchard to the rear of the house from which Rand looked.

===Chinese===
兰德望向窗外。弗莱明宅邸被雨浸透的草坪，往西约一百码处到了头；再过去，一座果园正开始抽芽绽叶；果园和另一方草坪之后，立着一幢半木结构的都铎式宅子，比弗莱明家的小着几号。一条小径从那宅子下来通到果园，另一条又从果园通到兰德眺望的这所宅子的后头。

===Original===
"Must be comforting to know your lawyer's so handy," he commented. "And what do you think, Walters? Are you satisfied, in your own mind, that Mr. Fleming was killed accidentally?"

===Chinese===
「律师住得这么近，想必叫人安心，」他评论道，「那么您自己怎么想，沃尔特斯？您打心眼里相信，弗莱明先生是意外丧生的吗？」

===Original===
The servant looked at him seriously. "No, sir; I'm not," he replied. "I've thought about it a great deal, since it happened, sir, and I just can't believe that Mr. Fleming would have that revolver, and start working on it, without knowing that it was loaded. That just isn't possible, if you'll pardon me, sir. And I can't understand how he would have shot himself while removing the charges. The fact is, when I came up here at quarter of seven, to call him for cocktails, he had the whole thing apart and spread out in front of him." The butler thought for a moment. "I believe Mr. Dunmore had something like that in mind when he called Mr. Goode."

===Chinese===
仆人神情郑重地望着他。「不，先生；我不信，」他答道，「自出事以来，我把这事想了又想，先生，我就是不能相信，弗莱明先生会拿着那支转轮枪动手拆弄，却不知它膛里有子弹。恕我直言，先生，这根本不可能。我也想不通，他退弹药时怎么会打中自己。事实是，七点差一刻我上楼来请他用鸡尾酒时，那支枪已被他拆了个七零八落，摊了满满一桌。管家想了想。「我相信，邓莫尔先生去请古德先生时，心里转的多少就是这样的念头。」

===Original===
"Well, what happened?" Rand asked. "Did the coroner or the doctor choke on calling it an accident?"

===Chinese===
「后来呢？」兰德问，「验尸官或者医生，在把这事裁成意外时，可曾卡壳作难？」

===Original===
"Oh no, sir; there was no trouble of any sort about that. You see, Dr. Yardman called the coroner, as soon as he arrived, but Mr. Goode was here already. He'd come over by that path you saw, to the rear of the house, and in through the garage, which was open, since Mrs. Dunmore was out with the coupé. They all talked it over for a while, and the coroner decided that there would be no need for any inquest, and the doctor wrote out the certificate. That was all there was to it."

===Chinese===
「哦，没有，先生；那上头没起半点波澜。您瞧，雅德曼医生（Dr. Yardman）一到便报了验尸官，可那时古德先生已经在场了。他是打您刚才望见的那条小径过来的，到宅子后头，再从车库进来——车库门开着，因为邓莫尔太太开双门轿车出门去了。几位先生商量了一阵，验尸官便认定无须开庭验尸，医生开出了证明。事情就这么了结了。」

===Original===
Rand looked at the section of pistol-rack devoted to Colts.

===Chinese===
兰德望向枪架上专挂柯尔特的那一片。

===Original===
"Which one was it?" he asked.

===Chinese===
「是哪一支？」他问。

===Original===
"Oh it's not here, sir," Walters replied. "The coroner took it away with him."

===Chinese===
「哦，它不在这儿了，先生，」沃尔特斯答道，「验尸官把它带走了。」

===Original===
"And hasn't returned it yet? Well, he has no business keeping it. It's part of the collection, and belongs to the estate."

===Chinese===
「还没送回来？哼，他无权扣着它。它是收藏的一部分，属于遗产。」

===Original===
"Yes, sir. If I may say so, I thought it was a bit high-handed of him, taking it away, myself, but it wasn't my place to say anything about it."

===Chinese===
「是的，先生。恕我多嘴，他那样把枪带走，我自己也觉得未免专断了些，只是那不是我该置喙的事。」

===Original===
"Well, I'll make it mine. If that revolver's what I'm told it is, it's too valuable to let some damned county-seat politician walk off with." A thought occurred to him. "And if I find that he's disposed of it, this county's going to need a new coroner, at least till the present incumbent gets out of jail."

===Chinese===
「那就换我来置喙。那支转轮枪若真如我所闻的那么值钱，可容不得哪个该死的县城政客顺手拎走。」他忽然想到一层。「倘若叫我发现他已把它处置掉了，本县就得另请一位验尸官了——至少也得等现在这位出了班房再说。」

===Original===
The buzzer of the extension phone went off like an annoyed rattlesnake. Walters scooped it up, spoke into it, listened for a moment, and handed it to Rand.

===Chinese===
分机的蜂鸣器像条被惹恼的响尾蛇似的叫将起来。沃尔特斯一把抓起话机，对着它说了句什么，听了片刻，递给兰德。

===Original===
"For you, sir; Mrs. Fleming."

===Chinese===
「您的电话，先生；弗莱明太太。」

===Original===
"Colonel Rand, Carl Gwinnett, the commission-dealer I told you about is here," Gladys told him. "Do you want to talk to him?"

===Chinese===
「兰德上校，我跟您提过的那位寄售商卡尔·格温内特来了，」格拉迪斯对他说，「您要见他吗？」

===Original===
"Why, yes. Do I understand, now, that you and the other ladies want cash, and don't want the collection peddled off piecemeal? … All right, send him up. I'll talk to him."

===Chinese===
「哦，要见。那么我这样理解对不对：您和其他几位太太要的是现钱，不愿这批收藏零零碎碎地零卖？……好，请他上来吧。我同他谈。」

===Original===
A few minutes later, a short, compact-looking man of forty-odd entered the gunroom, shifting a briefcase to his left hand and extending his right. Rand advanced to meet him and shook hands with him.

===Chinese===
几分钟后，一个矮墩结实、四十出头年纪的男人走进枪房，把公文包换到左手，伸出右手。兰德迎上前去，与他握了手。

===Original===
"You're Colonel Rand? Enjoyed your articles in the *Rifleman*," he said. "Mrs. Fleming tells me you're handling the sale of the collection for the estate."

===Chinese===
「您就是兰德上校？《步枪手》（Rifleman）上您那几篇文章，我读得津津有味，」他说，「弗莱明太太告诉我，您正替遗产经办这批收藏的出售。」

===Original===
"That's right, Mr. Gwinnett. Mrs. Fleming tells me you're interested."

===Chinese===
「正是，格温内特先生。弗莱明太太告诉我您有兴趣。」

===Original===
"Yes. Originally, I offered to sell the collection for her on a commission basis, but she didn't seem to care for the idea, and neither do the other ladies. They all want spot cash, in a lump sum."

===Chinese===
「是的。起初我提议替她寄售这批收藏，抽佣金办事，可她似乎不中意这个法子，其他几位太太也一样。她们都要现款，整笔付清。」

===Original===
"Yes. Mrs. Fleming herself might have been interested in your proposition, if she'd been sole owner. You could probably get more for the collection, even after deducting your commission, than I'll be able to, but the collection belongs to the estate, and has to be sold before any division can be made."

===Chinese===
「是了。弗莱明太太本人若是唯一产权人，对您那套办法倒未必不动心。扣去您的佣金，这批收藏您多半还能比我多卖出些价钱；无奈收藏属于遗产，总得先行售出，才谈得上分配。」

===Original===
"Yes, I see that. Well, how much would the estate, or you, consider a reasonable offer?"

===Chinese===
「是，这个我明白。那么，遗产一方，或者说您，觉得多少算个合理的出价？」

===Original===
"Sit down, Mr. Gwinnett," Rand invited. "What would you consider a reasonable offer, yourself? We're not asking any specific price; we're just taking bids, as it were."

===Chinese===
「请坐，格温内特先生，」兰德相邀，「您自己觉得多少算合理呢？我们没开什么实价；不妨说，我们只是广收出价。」

===Original===
"Well, how much have you been offered, to date?"

===Chinese===
「那么，到眼下为止，您已收到过多少出价？」

===Original===
"Well, we haven't heard from everybody. In fact, we haven't put out a list, or solicited offers, except locally, as yet. But one gentleman has expressed a willingness to pay up to twenty-five thousand dollars."

===Chinese===
「呃，还没问遍各方。其实，清单尚未发出，征求报价也还只在本地小试了试。不过有一位先生已表示，肯出到两万五千美元。」

===Original===
Gwinnett's face expressed polite skepticism. "Colonel Rand!" he protested. "You certainly don't take an offer like that seriously?"

===Chinese===
格温内特脸上浮起一层客气的怀疑。「兰德上校！」他抗议道，「这样的出价，您总不至于当真吧？」

===Original===
"I think it was made seriously," Rand replied. "A respectable profit could be made on the collection, even at that price."

===Chinese===
「我以为，人家出价时是认真的，」兰德答道，「即便照这个价吃进，这批收藏也还有一份体面的赚头。」

===Original===
Gwinnett's eyes shifted over the rows of horizontal barrels on the walls. He was almost visibly wrestling with mental arithmetic, and at the same time trying to keep any hint of his notion of the collection's real value out of his face.

===Chinese===
格温内特的目光扫过墙上一排排平置的枪管。他几乎是在明明白白地跟心算角力，一面又竭力不让半点关于这批收藏真实身价的念头爬上脸来。

===Original===
"Well, I doubt if I could raise that much," he said. "Might I ask who's making this offer?"

===Chinese===
「呃，我未必凑得出这么一大笔，」他说，「可否请问，出这个价的是哪位？」

===Original===
"You might; I'm afraid I couldn't tell you. You wouldn't want me to publish your own offer broadcast, would you?"

===Chinese===
「问是问得；只怕我不能奉告。换作您自己的出价，您也不愿我四处张扬吧？」

===Original===
"I think I can guess. If I'm right, don't hold your head in a tub of water till you get it," Gwinnett advised. "Making a big offer to scare away competition is one thing, and paying off on it is another. I've seen that happen before, you know. Fact is, there's one dealer, not far from here, who makes a regular habit of it. He'll make some fantastic offer, and then, when everybody's been bluffed out, he'll start making objections and finding faults, and before long he'll be down to about a quarter of his original price."

===Chinese===
「我想我猜得着。要是我没猜错，那笔钱到手之前，您可别把脑袋浸在水盆里干等，」格温内特进言道，「开个大价吓退竞争者是一回事，照价掏钱又是另一回事。这种事我从前见得多了，您知道。不瞒您说，离这儿不远就有个商人，干这个是老手了。他先开出个天价，等把众人都唬住了，便开始挑刺找岔，不出多久，价钱就杀到原先的四分之一上下。」

===Original===
"The practice isn't unknown," Rand admitted.

===Chinese===
「这路伎俩，倒也不是没人使过，」兰德承认道。

===Original===
"I'll bet you don't have this twenty-five thousand dollar offer on paper, over a signature," Gwinnett pursued. "Well, here." He opened his briefcase and extracted a sheet of paper, handing it to Rand. "You can file this; I'll stand back of it."

===Chinese===
「我敢打赌，那笔两万五的出价，您手上没有落着签名的书面凭据，」格温内特紧追不舍，「喏，这个给您。」他打开公文包，抽出一张纸，递给兰德。「这个您只管归档；我为它作保。」

===Original===
Rand looked at the typed and signed statement to the effect that Carl Gwinnett agreed to pay the sum of fifteen thousand dollars for the Lane Fleming pistol-collection, in its entirety, within thirty days of date. That was an average of six dollars a pistol. There had been a time, not too long ago, when a pistol-collection with an average value of six dollars, particularly one as large as the Fleming collection, had been something unusual. For one thing, arms values had increased sharply in the meantime. For another, Lane Fleming had kept his collection clean of the two-dollar items which dragged down so many collectors' average values. Except for the two-dozen-odd mysterious interlopers, there wasn't a pistol in the Fleming collection that wasn't worth at least twenty dollars, and quite a few had values expressible in three figures.

===Chinese===
兰德看着那份打字誊清、落款署名的声明，大意谓：卡尔·格温内特同意以一万五千美元总价，于三十日内买下莱恩·弗莱明手枪收藏的全部。折算下来，平均每支枪六美元。就在不算太久之前，一支平均身价六美元的手枪收藏——尤其是弗莱明这样规模的收藏——还算得上一桩稀罕物。一来，这期间古董枪身价早已暴涨。二来，莱恩·弗莱明守住了收藏的干净，没让那些两美元一档的地摊货进来拉低均价——多少藏家的平均身价，正是被这类货色拖垮的。除开那二十几件神秘的杂牌闯入货，弗莱明收藏里没有一支枪不值二十美元上下，身价须用三位数来表示的也为数不少。

===Original===
"Well, your offer is duly received and filed, Mr. Gwinnett," Rand told him, folding the sheet and putting it in his pocket. "This is better than an unwitnessed verbal statement that somebody is willing to pay twenty-five thousand. I'll certainly bear you in mind."

===Chinese===
「好，您的出价已如数收讫、归档存查，格温内特先生，」兰德对他说，把那张纸折好，放进口袋。「这可比一句无人在场的口头空话——说某人肯出两万五——强多了。我一定把您记在心上。」

===Original===
"You can show that to Arnold Rivers, if you want to," Gwinnett said. "See how much he's willing to commit himself to, over his signature."

===Chinese===
「您高兴的话，尽管拿这个给阿诺德·里弗斯看，」格温内特说，「瞧瞧他肯在签名底下把自己认到多少。」

## VIII / 八

===Original===
Pre-dinner cocktails in the library seemed to be a sort of household rite﻿—a self-imposed Truce of Bacchus before the resumption of hostilities in the dining-room. It lasted from six forty-five to seven; everybody sipped Manhattans and kept quiet and listened to the radio newscast. The only new face, to Rand, was Fred Dunmore’s.

===Chinese===
餐前在书房里喝鸡尾酒，似乎是这户人家的一种家常仪式——在餐厅重开战端之前自行订立的一段酒神休战（Truce of Bacchus）。仪式从六点三刻持续到七点；人人小口啜着曼哈顿鸡尾酒，安安静静，听着电台的新闻广播。在兰德眼里，唯一的新面孔，是弗雷德·邓莫尔的。

===Original===
It was a smooth, pinkly-shaven face, decorated with octagonal rimless glasses; an entirely unremarkable face; the face of the type that used to be labeled “Babbitt.” The corner of Rand’s mind that handled such data subconsciously filed his description: forty-five to fifty, one-eighty, five feet eight, hair brown and thinning, eyes blue. To this he added the Rotarian button on the lapel, and the small gold globule on the watch chain that testified that, when his age and weight had been considerably less, Dunmore had played on somebody’s basketball team. At that time he had probably belonged to the Y.M.C.A., and had thought that Mussolini was doing a splendid job in Italy, that H. L. Mencken ought to be deported to Russia, and that Prohibition was here to stay. At company sales meetings, he probably radiated an aura of synthetic good-fellowship.

===Chinese===
那是一张光洁、剃得泛粉的脸，缀着一副八边形无框眼镜；一张全然平平无奇的脸；一张过去会被冠上「巴比特」（Babbitt，辛克莱·刘易斯笔下的市侩典型）之名的那一类脸。兰德脑中专办此类资料的那个角落，已下意识地把他的底细归了档：四十五到五十岁，一百八十磅，身高五英尺八，棕发渐稀，蓝眼睛。在此之外，还可添上西服翻领上的扶轮社徽章，和表链上那颗小小的金珠——它证明邓莫尔在年纪和体重都还轻得多的当年，曾在某支篮球队里打过球。那个年头，他八成还是基督教青年会（Y.M.C.A.）的会员，而且认定墨索里尼在意大利干得有声有色，H·L·门肯（H. L. Mencken）理应驱逐到俄国去，禁酒令注定天长地久。在公司销售会议上，他八成周身散发着一股人工合成的热络劲儿。

===Original===
As Rand followed Walters down the spiral from the gunroom, the radio commercial was just starting, and Geraldine was asking Dunmore where Anton was.

===Chinese===
兰德跟着沃尔特斯从枪房顺螺旋楼梯下来时，收音机里的广告正好开播，杰拉尔丁正在问邓莫尔，安东到哪儿去了。

===Original===
“Oh, you know,” Dunmore told her, impatiently. “He had to go to Louisburg, to that Medical Association meeting; he’s reading a paper about the new diabetic ration.”

===Chinese===
「哦，你是知道的，」邓莫尔不耐烦地告诉她，「他得去路易斯堡（Louisburg），开那个医学会；他要宣读一篇论文，讲糖尿病人新的配给饮食。」

===Original===
He broke off as Rand approached and was introduced by Gladys, who handed both men their cocktails. Then the news commentator greeted them out of the radio, and everybody absorbed the day’s news along with their Manhattans. After the broadcast, they all crossed the hall to the dining-room, where hostilities began almost before the soup was cool enough to taste.

===Chinese===
兰德走近时，他把话打住，由格拉迪斯引见，两人握了手，她把鸡尾酒递到两位先生手里。随后新闻评论员从收音机里向他们道了问候，人人就着曼哈顿，把当天的新闻一并吸收下去。广播结束后，众人穿过门厅进了餐厅——汤还没凉到能入口，战事几乎已经打响。

===Original===
“I don’t see why you women had to do this,” Dunmore huffed. “Rivers has made us a fair offer. Bringing in an outsider will only give him the impression that we lack confidence in him.”

===Chinese===
「我不明白你们这些女人干吗非这么办不可，」邓莫尔怒冲冲地说，「里弗斯已经给了我们一个公道的报价。再拉个外人来，只会叫他觉得我们信不过他。」

===Original===
“Well, won’t that be just too, too bad!” Geraldine slashed at him. “We mustn’t ever hurt dear Mr. Rivers’s feelings like that. Let him have the collection for half what it’s worth, but never, never let him think we know what a Goddamned crook he is!”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，那岂不是太糟糕、太糟糕了！」杰拉尔丁迎头就砍，「咱们千万不能那样伤了亲爱的里弗斯先生的感情。把收藏半价白送给他都行，可千万、千万别叫他想到，我们心里明白他是个天杀的骗子！」

===Original===
Dunmore evidently didn’t think that worth dignifying with an answer. Doubtless he expected Nelda to launch a counteroffensive, as a matter of principle. If he did, he was disappointed.

===Chinese===
邓莫尔显然认为那话不值一驳。不用说，他指望内尔达出于原则发起反攻。倘若如此，他可是失望了。

===Original===
